import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
import datetime

import sys
sys.path.append("/Users/derekdewald/Documents/Python/Github_Repo/d_py_functions")
from objects_automated import object_dict
df = pd.read_excel('dict_blank.xlsx')

def apply_dictionary(
    df,
    new_column_name,
    mapping_dict,
    default_value='TBD',
    column_name="COLUMN_NAME",
):
    df = df.copy()
    conditions = [
        df[column_name].str.contains(key, na=False)
        for key in mapping_dict
    ]

    df[new_column_name] = np.select(
        conditions,
        mapping_dict.values(),
        default='default_value'
    )

product_map = {
    'DATE': 'Not a Product',
    'CK': 'Chequing',
    'SAV': 'Savings',
    'TD': 'Term Deposit',
    'LOAN': 'Loan',
    'LOC': 'LOC',
    'TFSA': 'TFSA',
    'RRSP': 'RRSP',
    'HISA': 'HISA',
    'FHSA': 'FHSA',
    'REGISTERED': 'Registered',
    'BP': 'Bill Payment',
    'BC': 'Bank Card',
    'DEBIT': 'Debit',
    'CREDIT': 'Credit',
    'EMAIL': 'Email',
    'INTERNET': 'Internet',
    'EFT': 'EFT',
    'TXN': 'Transaction',
}

apply_dictionary(df,'PRODUCT',product_map,'Not a Product')
df = pd.read_excel('dict_blank.xlsx')

def apply_dictionary(
    df,
    new_column_name,
    mapping_dict,
    default_value='TBD',
    column_name="COLUMN_NAME",
):
    df = df.copy()
    conditions = [
        df[column_name].str.contains(key, na=False)
        for key in mapping_dict
    ]

    df[new_column_name] = np.select(
        conditions,
        mapping_dict.values(),
        default=default_value
    )
    return df

product_map = {
    'DATE': 'Not a Product',
    'CK': 'Chequing',
    'SAV': 'Savings',
    'TD': 'Term Deposit',
    'LOAN': 'Loan',
    'LOC': 'LOC',
    'TFSA': 'TFSA',
    'RRSP': 'RRSP',
    'HISA': 'HISA',
    'FHSA': 'FHSA',
    'REGISTERED': 'Registered',
    'BP': 'Bill Payment',
    'BC': 'Bank Card',
    'DEBIT': 'Debit',
    'CREDIT': 'Credit',
    'EMAIL': 'Email',
    'INTERNET': 'Internet',
    'EFT': 'EFT',
    'TXN': 'Transaction',
}

df = apply_dictionary(df,'PRODUCT',product_map,'Not a Product')

metric_dict = {
    'BAL':"Balance",
    'VAL':'Value',
    'VOL':"Volumne"
}

df = apply_dictionary(df,'METRIC',metric_dict,'Not a Metric')
df['METRIC'] = np.where((df['PRODUCT']!='Not a Product'))&(df['METRIC']!='Not a Metric')),'Count',df['METRIC'])

df[df['METRIC']=='Not a Metric']
df = pd.read_excel('dict_blank.xlsx')

    'DATE': 'Not a Product',
    'CK': 'Chequing',

def apply_dictionary(
    df,
    new_column_name,
    mapping_dict,
    default_value='TBD',
    column_name="COLUMN_NAME",
):
    df = df.copy()
    conditions = [
        df[column_name].str.contains(key, na=False)
        for key in mapping_dict
    ]

    df[new_column_name] = np.select(
        conditions,
        mapping_dict.values(),
        default=default_value
    )
    return df

def blank_dict_ml_project():
    df = pd.read_excel('/Users/derekdewald/Documents/Python/Github_Repo/Streamlit/Data/knowledge_base.xlsx')

    return {x:"" for x in df[(df['Process'].str.contains('Machine Learning Lifecycle'))&(df['Categorization']=='Process Step')]['Word']}

project_dict = blank_dict_ml_project()
project_dict
project_dict['Goal Setting'] = {
    'Business Need':'Growth Marketing needs a faster, reusable predictive capability for campaign targeting because the current process relies too heavily on broad segmentation/generalizations when there is not time to build bespoke analytics.',
    'Challenge':" To develop a meaningful and highly effective customer we will need granular customer level information, of high quality, consistency and overtime. At this time the availability of such information is not robust, such the most effective approach is to likely focus on models which we can implement, and then leverage them for approximatation, opposed to explicitness.',
    'Approach':"""
    3 Project Phases. 
    Phase 1, Develop 3 Models. 
    1) Generalized Engagement Model - Clustering Model Based on Product Holdings and Change Overtime. Should these be 2 different Models, or 1?
    2) Client Opportunity Model - Based on Net Worth, Financial Holdings, Demographics. 
    3) Next Best Product - Based on Models, age, etc can we approximate what a member is missing.
    
    Phase 2, Communicate Models to Business, be clear of what they are, and how they have been put togehter. 
    1) Reconcile Model Output with Historical Performance. 
    2) Understand performance demographics/segments, determine how this can impact model design.
    3) Understand what information might be necessary to tailor more granulary at a member level. 

    Phase 3. 
    1) Generate More Complete and Complex Evaluation Paradigm
    2) Communicate Results and Finding to Business for education, enforcement, awareness, to push operational change.
    
    """
}
project_dict['Goal Setting'] = {
    'Business Need':'Growth Marketing needs a faster, reusable predictive capability for campaign targeting because the current process relies too heavily on broad segmentation/generalizations when there is not time to build bespoke analytics.',
    'Challenge':'To develop a meaningful and highly effective customer we will need granular customer level information, of high quality, consistency and overtime. At this time the availability of such information is not robust, such the most effective approach is to likely focus on models which we can implement, and then leverage them for approximatation, opposed to explicitness.',
    'Approach':"""
    3 Project Phases. 
    Phase 1, Develop 3 Models. 
    1) Generalized Engagement Model - Clustering Model Based on Product Holdings and Change Overtime. Should these be 2 different Models, or 1?
    2) Client Opportunity Model - Based on Net Worth, Financial Holdings, Demographics. 
    3) Next Best Product - Based on Models, age, etc can we approximate what a member is missing.
    
    Phase 2, Communicate Models to Business, be clear of what they are, and how they have been put togehter. 
    1) Reconcile Model Output with Historical Performance. 
    2) Understand performance demographics/segments, determine how this can impact model design.
    3) Understand what information might be necessary to tailor more granulary at a member level. 

    Phase 3. 
    1) Generate More Complete and Complex Evaluation Paradigm
    2) Communicate Results and Finding to Business for education, enforcement, awareness, to push operational change.
    
    """
}
project_dict['Goal Setting'] = {
    
    'Business Need': '''
    Growth Marketing needs a faster, reusable predictive capability for campaign 
    targeting because the current process relies too heavily on broad segmentation 
    and generalizations when there is insufficient time to develop bespoke analytics.
    ''',
   'Goal': '''
    Develop and evaluate a generalized member intelligence capability that improves 
    Growth Marketing's ability to identify meaningful member and product opportunities 
    using available data, while establishing a reusable analytical foundation for 
    increasingly granular and effective campaign targeting.
    ''',
    'Deliverable': '''
    Develop and validate a generalized analytical framework consisting of:
    
    1) an Engagement Model that describes the strength, depth, and trajectory of the 
       member relationship;
    2) an Opportunity Model that estimates the unrealized opportunity represented by 
       the member relationship; and
    3) a Product Opportunity Model that combines these and other relevant information 
       to identify potentially relevant product opportunities.

    The framework will be evaluated against historical member behaviour to determine 
    its effectiveness, limitations, and potential application to campaign targeting. 
    Findings will be communicated to Growth Marketing together with recommendations 
    for the data and operational capabilities required to improve future predictive 
    targeting.
    ''',
    'Challenge': '''
    Developing highly effective member-level predictive models requires granular, 
    high-quality, consistent, and longitudinal member information. At present, the 
    availability and robustness of this information is limited.

    Rather than creating a level of precision that the available data cannot reliably 
    support, the project will focus on developing generalized analytical models using 
    information currently available. These models will provide progressively greater 
    analytical resolution while also identifying the additional data, information, 
    and operational capabilities required to support more granular member-level 
    prediction in the future.
    ''',

    'Approach': {

        'Phase 1 - Generalized Member Intelligence': {

            'Engagement Model': '''
            Develop a generalized view of member engagement using observable product 
            holdings, relationship breadth and depth, behavioural activity, and change 
            over time.

            Explore whether these dimensions can be represented within a single 
            engagement model or whether distinct components are required. Clustering 
            is an initial candidate methodology, but the analytical objective is to 
            identify meaningful and interpretable engagement profiles rather than to 
            prescribe a specific algorithm.
            ''',

            'Opportunity Model': '''
            Develop a generalized view of the unrealized opportunity represented by 
            each member relationship.

            Explore the relationship between estimated financial capacity, financial 
            holdings, the member's existing relationship with the organization, and 
            other relevant contextual information. The model should distinguish 
            observable information from inferred opportunity and avoid overstating 
            the precision supported by the available data.
            ''',

            'Product Opportunity Model': '''
            Use Engagement, Opportunity, existing product holdings, behavioural 
            information, and relevant constraints to identify products that appear 
            underrepresented or potentially relevant within the member relationship.

            Initially treat this as a Product Opportunity or Product Gap model rather 
            than a true Next Best Product model. Historical validation will determine 
            whether identified product opportunities are sufficiently associated with 
            subsequent product adoption or growth to support a Next Best Product 
            capability.
            '''
        },

        'Phase 2 - Validation and Learning': [
            '''
            Reconcile model outputs with historical member behaviour and performance 
            to determine whether the generalized models contain meaningful predictive 
            information.
            ''',
            '''
            Evaluate performance across relevant member profiles, demographics, 
            segments, and other characteristics to understand where the models perform 
            well, where they perform poorly, and whether model design should change.
            ''',
            '''
            Identify information that is unavailable or insufficiently robust and 
            determine what additional data would be required to support more granular 
            and reliable member-level prediction.
            '''
        ],

        'Phase 3 - Capability Advancement': [
            '''
            Develop a more complete evaluation paradigm for assessing predictive 
            performance, campaign outcomes, incremental value, model limitations, and 
            opportunities for continued improvement.
            ''',
            '''
            Translate analytical findings into clear business insights that explain 
            what the models can and cannot determine and how they can appropriately 
            support campaign targeting.
            ''',
            '''
            Identify and communicate the data, process, measurement, and operational 
            changes required to progress from generalized member intelligence toward 
            more granular predictive targeting and a mature Next Best Product 
            capability.
            '''
        ]
    },
    'Success Criteria': {

    'Analytical': '''
    Demonstrate that the generalized models produce stable, interpretable, and 
    meaningfully differentiated member profiles and opportunities that can be 
    reconciled with observable historical member behaviour.

    In the absence of an established analytical baseline, initial success will focus 
    on demonstrating meaningful signal, consistency, and practical interpretability 
    rather than improvement against an existing model.
    ''',

    'Business Adoption': '''
    Determine whether Growth Marketing understands, trusts, and finds sufficient 
    practical value in the analytical capability to use it in campaign planning, 
    targeting, evaluation, or subsequent analytical requests.

    Adoption and repeated use will represent an important measure of success given 
    that the capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to 
    an established business requirement.
    ''',

    'Capability Development': '''
    Establish a clearer understanding of the data, measurement, analytical, and 
    operational capabilities required to support increasingly granular predictive 
    campaign targeting.

    Success includes explicitly identifying limitations in the current environment 
    and translating those limitations into actionable requirements for future 
    capability development.
    '''
}

    Determine whether Growth Marketing understands, trusts, and finds sufficient 
    practical value in the analytical capability to use it in campaign planning, 
    targeting, evaluation, or subsequent analytical requests.

    Adoption and repeated use will represent an important measure of success given 
    that the capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to 
    an established business requirement.
    ''',

    'Capability Development': '''
    Establish a clearer understanding of the data, measurement, analytical, and 
    operational capabilities required to support increasingly granular predictive 
    campaign targeting.

    Success includes explicitly identifying limitations in the current environment 
    and translating those limitations into actionable requirements for future 
    capability development.
    '''
    }
}
project_dict['Goal Setting'] = {
    
    'Business Need': '''
    Growth Marketing needs a faster, reusable predictive capability for campaign 
    targeting because the current process relies too heavily on broad segmentation 
    and generalizations when there is insufficient time to develop bespoke analytics.
    ''',
    'Articulate Opportunity':"""
    Current state: Campaign launched → results happen → limited ability to establish what worked.
    Desired state: Campaign hypothesis → target definition → pre-campaign baseline → treatment/control where feasible → campaign → outcome window → incremental measurement → learning.
    ''',
   'Goal': '''
    Develop and evaluate a generalized member intelligence capability that improves 
    Growth Marketing's ability to identify meaningful member and product opportunities 
    using available data, while establishing a reusable analytical foundation for 
    increasingly granular and effective campaign targeting.
    ''',
    'Deliverable': '''
    Develop and validate a generalized analytical framework consisting of:
    
    1) an Engagement Model that describes the strength, depth, and trajectory of the 
       member relationship;
    2) an Opportunity Model that estimates the unrealized opportunity represented by 
       the member relationship; and
    3) a Product Opportunity Model that combines these and other relevant information 
       to identify potentially relevant product opportunities.

    The framework will be evaluated against historical member behaviour to determine 
    its effectiveness, limitations, and potential application to campaign targeting. 
    Findings will be communicated to Growth Marketing together with recommendations 
    for the data and operational capabilities required to improve future predictive 
    targeting.
    ''',
    'Challenge': '''
    Developing highly effective member-level predictive models requires granular, 
    high-quality, consistent, and longitudinal member information. At present, the 
    availability and robustness of this information is limited.

    Rather than creating a level of precision that the available data cannot reliably 
    support, the project will focus on developing generalized analytical models using 
    information currently available. These models will provide progressively greater 
    analytical resolution while also identifying the additional data, information, 
    and operational capabilities required to support more granular member-level 
    prediction in the future.
    ''',

    'Approach': {

        'Phase 1 - Generalized Member Intelligence': {

            'Engagement Model': '''
            Develop a generalized view of member engagement using observable product 
            holdings, relationship breadth and depth, behavioural activity, and change 
            over time.

            Explore whether these dimensions can be represented within a single 
            engagement model or whether distinct components are required. Clustering 
            is an initial candidate methodology, but the analytical objective is to 
            identify meaningful and interpretable engagement profiles rather than to 
            prescribe a specific algorithm.
            ''',

            'Opportunity Model': '''
            Develop a generalized view of the unrealized opportunity represented by 
            each member relationship.

            Explore the relationship between estimated financial capacity, financial 
            holdings, the member's existing relationship with the organization, and 
            other relevant contextual information. The model should distinguish 
            observable information from inferred opportunity and avoid overstating 
            the precision supported by the available data.
            ''',

            'Product Opportunity Model': '''
            Use Engagement, Opportunity, existing product holdings, behavioural 
            information, and relevant constraints to identify products that appear 
            underrepresented or potentially relevant within the member relationship.

            Initially treat this as a Product Opportunity or Product Gap model rather 
            than a true Next Best Product model. Historical validation will determine 
            whether identified product opportunities are sufficiently associated with 
            subsequent product adoption or growth to support a Next Best Product 
            capability.
            '''
        },

        'Phase 2 - Validation and Learning': [
            '''
            Reconcile model outputs with historical member behaviour and performance 
            to determine whether the generalized models contain meaningful predictive 
            information.
            ''',
            '''
            Evaluate performance across relevant member profiles, demographics, 
            segments, and other characteristics to understand where the models perform 
            well, where they perform poorly, and whether model design should change.
            ''',
            '''
            Identify information that is unavailable or insufficiently robust and 
            determine what additional data would be required to support more granular 
            and reliable member-level prediction.
            '''
        ],

        'Phase 3 - Capability Advancement': [
            '''
            Develop a more complete evaluation paradigm for assessing predictive 
            performance, campaign outcomes, incremental value, model limitations, and 
            opportunities for continued improvement.
            ''',
            '''
            Translate analytical findings into clear business insights that explain 
            what the models can and cannot determine and how they can appropriately 
            support campaign targeting.
            ''',
            '''
            Identify and communicate the data, process, measurement, and operational 
            changes required to progress from generalized member intelligence toward 
            more granular predictive targeting and a mature Next Best Product 
            capability.
            '''
        ]
    },
    'Success Criteria': {

    'Analytical': '''
    Demonstrate that the generalized models produce stable, interpretable, and 
    meaningfully differentiated member profiles and opportunities that can be 
    reconciled with observable historical member behaviour.

    In the absence of an established analytical baseline, initial success will focus 
    on demonstrating meaningful signal, consistency, and practical interpretability 
    rather than improvement against an existing model.
    ''',

    'Business Adoption': '''
    Determine whether Growth Marketing understands, trusts, and finds sufficient 
    practical value in the analytical capability to use it in campaign planning, 
    targeting, evaluation, or subsequent analytical requests.

    Adoption and repeated use will represent an important measure of success given 
    that the capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to 
    an established business requirement.
    ''',

    'Capability Development': '''
    Establish a clearer understanding of the data, measurement, analytical, and 
    operational capabilities required to support increasingly granular predictive 
    campaign targeting.

    Success includes explicitly identifying limitations in the current environment 
    and translating those limitations into actionable requirements for future 
    capability development.
    '''
    }
}

defintion_dict = {
'Relationship Breadth','The extent to which a member maintains relationships across different products, services, and capabilities offered by the organization. Breadth reflects the range of the relationship rather than the financial size or intensity of any individual component. How broad is the member's relationship with us?',
'Relationship Depth','The extent to which the member meaningfully uses or maintains the products and services they hold. Depth distinguishes merely having a relationship from having a substantive relationship within those products or services. How deeply does the member use what they have with us?',
'Behaviour','The frequency, consistency, and nature of the member's observable interactions with the organization. This captures how actively the member uses the organization as part of their ongoing financial activity. How actively does the member interact with us?',
'Trajectory','The direction and evolution of the member's relationship over time. Trajectory captures whether engagement appears to be strengthening, stable, weakening, or otherwise changing rather than relying only on the member's current state.  How is the member's relationship with us changing?'
}

How is the member's relationship with us changing?",
    "Current Known Financial Profile":"Represents the member's financial position as it can currently be observed or reasonably estimated from available information, including known financial holdings, wealth indicators, and their existing financial relationship with the organization. It provides a structured representation of what is currently known about the member's financial position while explicitly recognizing that the information may be incomplete, estimated, or subject to data-quality limitations. It should not be interpreted as a complete representation of the member's wealth, financial capacity, needs, or intentions. What do we currently know about the member's financial position?",
"Member Individuality Profile":"Represents a structured and consistently captured set of information about characteristics that distinguish individual members and are relevant and appropriate within the context of the financial relationship. This may include preferences, financial aspirations and priorities, communication and relationship preferences, relevant interests and values, observable behaviours, and other factors that may influence how a member engages with the organization and makes financial decisions. The Member Individuality Profile seeks to improve understanding of meaningful differences between members while deliberately limiting information to that which is appropriate and relevant in the normal course of business. It recognizes that any structured representation necessarily provides only a partial view of the complexity and uniqueness of an individual. What relevant and appropriate information do we know about what makes this member distinct as an individual?"
}
project_dict['Problem Definition'] = {

    'Project Problem': '''
    Growth Marketing requires a more reusable and analytically grounded approach to 
    identifying meaningful member and product opportunities. Current targeting is 
    constrained by limited time for bespoke analysis and by gaps in the availability, 
    consistency, and granularity of member-level information.

    The project will explore whether generalized member intelligence constructs can be 
    developed from currently available data and used to support more effective campaign 
    targeting, while explicitly identifying where additional data or analytical capability 
    is required.
    ''',

    'Analytical Problems': {

        'Engagement': {
            'Definition': '''
            Represents the extent and nature of a member's observable relationship with 
            the organization across the breadth of products and services they hold, the 
            depth with which they use those relationships, their ongoing behavioural 
            activity, and how that relationship is changing over time.

            Engagement reflects the strength and activity of the relationship rather than 
            the member's financial value, potential opportunity, satisfaction, loyalty, 
            or intent. How engaged is the member in their relationship with the organization?
            ''',

            'Problem Statement': '''
            Develop a generalized and interpretable representation of member engagement 
            using observable information about relationship breadth, relationship depth, 
            behavioural activity, and trajectory over time.

            The resulting representation should distinguish meaningfully different forms 
            and levels of engagement without conflating engagement with financial value 
            or estimated opportunity.
            '''
        },

        'Opportunity': {
            'Definition': "Develop a Current Known Financial Profile for each member using available information about their financial position, holdings, and relationship with the organization, and use that profile to identify and quantify potential financial opportunity. Opportunity may include both Known External Opportunity, where financial holdings outside the organization can be reasonably quantified, and Inferred Opportunity, where financial capacity or holdings appear likely to exist but are not directly evident within available information. The model should clearly distinguish observed, estimated, and inferred information and communicate the associated uncertainty rather than treating all identified opportunity as equally known. Based on the member's Current Known Financial Profile, what financial opportunity can we reasonably identify or infer beyond their existing relationship with us?",
            'Notes':"For the Scope of this we will focus on Current Known Financial Profile, not Member Individuality, which needs to be build on its own",
            'Problem Statement': 'TBD',
            'taxonomy':'Observed with us ↓ Known externally ↓ Estimated externally ↓ Inferred / unexplained opportunity'
        },

        'Product Opportunity': {
            'Definition': 'TBD',
            'Canonical Question': 'TBD',
            'Problem Statement': """
            Our existing relationship with a member provides only a partial view of their 
            overall financial position, making it difficult to determine whether their 
            current holdings with the organization represent the majority of their financial 
            relationship or whether meaningful financial opportunity exists elsewhere or 
            remains unexplained. A consistent approach is required to construct the Current 
            Known Financial Profile and distinguish between financial opportunity that is 
            known, estimated, or inferred.
    """
        }
    },

    'Key Questions': [
        '''
        Can observable member information be organized into stable and interpretable 
        representations of engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity?
        ''',
        '''
        Do these generalized constructs correspond with meaningful differences in 
        historical member behaviour?
        ''',
        '''
        Can these constructs provide sufficient analytical value to support campaign 
        targeting without requiring a bespoke predictive model for every campaign?
        ''',
        '''
        What additional information would materially improve the granularity, reliability, 
        or predictive usefulness of the resulting capability?
        '''
    ],

    'Assumptions': [
        '''
        Engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity are related but distinct 
        constructs and should not be treated as interchangeable measures.
        ''',
        '''
        The available data contains sufficient observable behavioural and relationship 
        information to develop useful generalized representations, even if it does not 
        support precise member-level prediction.
        ''',
        '''
        Historical member behaviour can provide evidence of whether the generalized 
        constructs contain meaningful signal, despite the absence of an established 
        business-performance baseline.
        '''
    ],

    'Constraints': [
        '''
        Member-level data is currently limited in granularity, consistency, quality, and 
        longitudinal depth.
        ''',
        '''
        There is no established organizational measurement paradigm or predictive baseline 
        against which model performance can initially be compared.
        ''',
        '''
        The capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to a fully 
        defined business requirement, making adoption and practical use important measures 
        of success.
        ''',
        '''
        Model outputs must not imply greater precision, causality, intent, loyalty, or 
        financial knowledge than can be supported by the available data.
        '''
    ]
}

    The project will explore whether generalized member intelligence constructs can be 
    developed from currently available data and used to support more effective campaign 
    targeting, while explicitly identifying where additional data or analytical capability 
    is required.
    ''',

    'Analytical Problems': {

        'Engagement': {
            'Definition': '''
            Represents the extent and nature of a member's observable relationship with 
            the organization across the breadth of products and services they hold, the 
            depth with which they use those relationships, their ongoing behavioural 
            activity, and how that relationship is changing over time.

            Engagement reflects the strength and activity of the relationship rather than 
            the member's financial value, potential opportunity, satisfaction, loyalty, 
            or intent. How engaged is the member in their relationship with the organization?
            ''',

            'Problem Statement': '''
            Develop a generalized and interpretable representation of member engagement 
            using observable information about relationship breadth, relationship depth, 
            behavioural activity, and trajectory over time.

            The resulting representation should distinguish meaningfully different forms 
            and levels of engagement without conflating engagement with financial value 
            or estimated opportunity.
            '''
        },

        'Opportunity': {
            'Definition': "Develop a Current Known Financial Profile for each member using available information about their financial position, holdings, and relationship with the organization, and use that profile to identify and quantify potential financial opportunity. Opportunity may include both Known External Opportunity, where financial holdings outside the organization can be reasonably quantified, and Inferred Opportunity, where financial capacity or holdings appear likely to exist but are not directly evident within available information. The model should clearly distinguish observed, estimated, and inferred information and communicate the associated uncertainty rather than treating all identified opportunity as equally known. Based on the member's Current Known Financial Profile, what financial opportunity can we reasonably identify or infer beyond their existing relationship with us?",
            'Notes':"For the Scope of this we will focus on Current Known Financial Profile, not Member Individuality, which needs to be build on its own",
            'taxonomy':'Observed with us ↓ Known externally ↓ Estimated externally ↓ Inferred / unexplained opportunity',
            'Problem Statement': """
            Our existing relationship with a member provides only a partial view of their 
            overall financial position, making it difficult to determine whether their 
            current holdings with the organization represent the majority of their financial 
            relationship or whether meaningful financial opportunity exists elsewhere or 
            remains unexplained. A consistent approach is required to construct the Current 
            Known Financial Profile and distinguish between financial opportunity that is 
            known, estimated, or inferred.
    """
        },

        'Product Opportunity': {
            'Definition': '''
            Identifies products that may represent a meaningful opportunity to deepen a 
            member's relationship with the organization based on their existing product 
            holdings, Engagement, Current Known Financial Profile, identified financial 
            Opportunity, and comparison with relevant peer groups.
            
            Peer comparison may consider characteristics such as age, geography, financial 
            profile, wealth, and other relevant observable characteristics to identify patterns 
            in the products held by members with reasonably similar profiles.
            
            Product Opportunity represents an indication of where opportunity may exist rather 
            than a prediction of member preference, intent, or likelihood to acquire a specific 
            product. Similarity to other members and the presence of an apparent product gap 
            should therefore be treated as evidence of potential opportunity rather than evidence 
            of member need or desire.
            
            Based on what we currently know about the member and what we 
            observe among reasonably similar members, where does a meaningful product opportunity 
            appear to exist?
            ''',
                'Problem Statement': '''
            Existing product holdings and broad member characteristics provide an incomplete
            basis for determining where meaningful product opportunities may exist. Even where
            financial opportunity can be identified or inferred, there is currently no
            consistent approach for determining where within the member's product relationship
            that opportunity may be relevant.
        
            A method is needed to combine what is known about the individual member with
            patterns observed among reasonably similar members, while recognizing that product
            gaps or peer similarity do not in themselves demonstrate member need, preference,
            or intent.
            ''',
        
            'Opportunity': '''
            Develop a generalized and reusable capability for identifying and prioritizing
            potential product opportunities by combining member-level information with
            comparative patterns observed across similar members.
        
            This capability could provide Growth Marketing with a more analytically grounded
            starting point for campaign targeting than broad segmentation alone, while creating
            a foundation that can become increasingly individualized as richer information
            about member preferences, needs, behaviours, and Member Individuality becomes
            available.
            '''

        }
    },

    'Key Questions': [
        '''
        Can observable member information be organized into stable and interpretable 
        representations of engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity?
        ''',
        '''
        Do these generalized constructs correspond with meaningful differences in 
        historical member behaviour?
        ''',
        '''
        Can these constructs provide sufficient analytical value to support campaign 
        targeting without requiring a bespoke predictive model for every campaign?
        ''',
        '''
        What additional information would materially improve the granularity, reliability, 
        or predictive usefulness of the resulting capability?
        '''
    ],

    'Assumptions': [
        '''
        Engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity are related but distinct 
        constructs and should not be treated as interchangeable measures.
        ''',
        '''
        The available data contains sufficient observable behavioural and relationship 
        information to develop useful generalized representations, even if it does not 
        support precise member-level prediction.
        ''',
        '''
        Historical member behaviour can provide evidence of whether the generalized 
        constructs contain meaningful signal, despite the absence of an established 
        business-performance baseline.
        '''
    ],

    'Constraints': [
        '''
        Member-level data is currently limited in granularity, consistency, quality, and 
        longitudinal depth.
        ''',
        '''
        There is no established organizational measurement paradigm or predictive baseline 
        against which model performance can initially be compared.
        ''',
        '''
        The capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to a fully 
        defined business requirement, making adoption and practical use important measures 
        of success.
        ''',
        '''
        Model outputs must not imply greater precision, causality, intent, loyalty, or 
        financial knowledge than can be supported by the available data.
        '''
    ]
}

## Need to change the process of Data Collection

dc = {
"Semantic Classification":'Purpose: Establish a consistent understanding of what information is contained within the available dataset. Canonical Question:What variables are contained in the dataset, and what do they represent?'
"Problem Decomposition":"Break the analytical problem into the distinct conceptual components that must be understood or represented to adequately address it. Canonical Question: What components are required to adequately represent the analytical problem?",
}

# Identify purpose of model and components it needs.
## Need to change the process of Data Collection

dc = {
"Semantic Classification":'Purpose: Establish a consistent understanding of what information is contained within the available dataset. Canonical Question:What variables are contained in the dataset, and what do they represent?',
"Problem Decomposition":"Break the analytical problem into the distinct conceptual components that must be understood or represented to adequately address it. Canonical Question: What components are required to adequately represent the analytical problem?",
}

# Identify purpose of model and components it needs.
project_dict['Goal Setting'] = {
    
    'Business Need': '''
    Growth Marketing needs a faster, reusable predictive capability for campaign 
    targeting because the current process relies too heavily on broad segmentation 
    and generalizations when there is insufficient time to develop bespoke analytics.
    ''',
    'Articulate Opportunity':"""
    Current state: Campaign launched → results happen → limited ability to establish what worked.
    Desired state: Campaign hypothesis → target definition → pre-campaign baseline → treatment/control where feasible → campaign → outcome window → incremental measurement → learning.
    """,
   'Goal': '''
    Develop and evaluate a generalized member intelligence capability that improves 
    Growth Marketing's ability to identify meaningful member and product opportunities 
    using available data, while establishing a reusable analytical foundation for 
    increasingly granular and effective campaign targeting.
    ''',
    'Deliverable': '''
    Develop and validate a generalized analytical framework consisting of:
    
    1) an Engagement Model that describes the strength, depth, and trajectory of the 
       member relationship;
    2) an Opportunity Model that estimates the unrealized opportunity represented by 
       the member relationship; and
    3) a Product Opportunity Model that combines these and other relevant information 
       to identify potentially relevant product opportunities.

    The framework will be evaluated against historical member behaviour to determine 
    its effectiveness, limitations, and potential application to campaign targeting. 
    Findings will be communicated to Growth Marketing together with recommendations 
    for the data and operational capabilities required to improve future predictive 
    targeting.
    ''',
    'Challenge': '''
    Developing highly effective member-level predictive models requires granular, 
    high-quality, consistent, and longitudinal member information. At present, the 
    availability and robustness of this information is limited.

    Rather than creating a level of precision that the available data cannot reliably 
    support, the project will focus on developing generalized analytical models using 
    information currently available. These models will provide progressively greater 
    analytical resolution while also identifying the additional data, information, 
    and operational capabilities required to support more granular member-level 
    prediction in the future.
    ''',

    'Approach': {

        'Phase 1 - Generalized Member Intelligence': {

            'Engagement Model': '''
            Develop a generalized view of member engagement using observable product 
            holdings, relationship breadth and depth, behavioural activity, and change 
            over time.

            Explore whether these dimensions can be represented within a single 
            engagement model or whether distinct components are required. Clustering 
            is an initial candidate methodology, but the analytical objective is to 
            identify meaningful and interpretable engagement profiles rather than to 
            prescribe a specific algorithm.
            ''',

            'Opportunity Model': '''
            Develop a generalized view of the unrealized opportunity represented by 
            each member relationship.

            Explore the relationship between estimated financial capacity, financial 
            holdings, the member's existing relationship with the organization, and 
            other relevant contextual information. The model should distinguish 
            observable information from inferred opportunity and avoid overstating 
            the precision supported by the available data.
            ''',

            'Product Opportunity Model': '''
            Use Engagement, Opportunity, existing product holdings, behavioural 
            information, and relevant constraints to identify products that appear 
            underrepresented or potentially relevant within the member relationship.

            Initially treat this as a Product Opportunity or Product Gap model rather 
            than a true Next Best Product model. Historical validation will determine 
            whether identified product opportunities are sufficiently associated with 
            subsequent product adoption or growth to support a Next Best Product 
            capability.
            '''
        },

        'Phase 2 - Validation and Learning': [
            '''
            Reconcile model outputs with historical member behaviour and performance 
            to determine whether the generalized models contain meaningful predictive 
            information.
            ''',
            '''
            Evaluate performance across relevant member profiles, demographics, 
            segments, and other characteristics to understand where the models perform 
            well, where they perform poorly, and whether model design should change.
            ''',
            '''
            Identify information that is unavailable or insufficiently robust and 
            determine what additional data would be required to support more granular 
            and reliable member-level prediction.
            '''
        ],

        'Phase 3 - Capability Advancement': [
            '''
            Develop a more complete evaluation paradigm for assessing predictive 
            performance, campaign outcomes, incremental value, model limitations, and 
            opportunities for continued improvement.
            ''',
            '''
            Translate analytical findings into clear business insights that explain 
            what the models can and cannot determine and how they can appropriately 
            support campaign targeting.
            ''',
            '''
            Identify and communicate the data, process, measurement, and operational 
            changes required to progress from generalized member intelligence toward 
            more granular predictive targeting and a mature Next Best Product 
            capability.
            '''
        ]
    },
    'Success Criteria': {

    'Analytical': '''
    Demonstrate that the generalized models produce stable, interpretable, and 
    meaningfully differentiated member profiles and opportunities that can be 
    reconciled with observable historical member behaviour.

    In the absence of an established analytical baseline, initial success will focus 
    on demonstrating meaningful signal, consistency, and practical interpretability 
    rather than improvement against an existing model.
    ''',

    'Business Adoption': '''
    Determine whether Growth Marketing understands, trusts, and finds sufficient 
    practical value in the analytical capability to use it in campaign planning, 
    targeting, evaluation, or subsequent analytical requests.

    Adoption and repeated use will represent an important measure of success given 
    that the capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to 
    an established business requirement.
    ''',

    'Capability Development': '''
    Establish a clearer understanding of the data, measurement, analytical, and 
    operational capabilities required to support increasingly granular predictive 
    campaign targeting.

    Success includes explicitly identifying limitations in the current environment 
    and translating those limitations into actionable requirements for future 
    capability development.
    '''
    }
}    

project_dict['Problem Definition'] = {

    'Project Problem': '''
    Growth Marketing requires a more reusable and analytically grounded approach to 
    identifying meaningful member and product opportunities. Current targeting is 
    constrained by limited time for bespoke analysis and by gaps in the availability, 
    consistency, and granularity of member-level information.

    The project will explore whether generalized member intelligence constructs can be 
    developed from currently available data and used to support more effective campaign 
    targeting, while explicitly identifying where additional data or analytical capability 
    is required.
    ''',

    'Analytical Problems': {

        'Engagement': {
            'Definition': '''
            Represents the extent and nature of a member's observable relationship with 
            the organization across the breadth of products and services they hold, the

            behavioural activity, and trajectory over time.

            and levels of engagement without conflating engagement with financial value 
            or estimated opportunity.

            patterns observed among reasonably similar members, while recognizing that product
            gaps or peer similarity do not in themselves demonstrate member need, preference,
            or intent.
            ''',
        
            'Opportunity': '''
            Develop a generalized and reusable capability for identifying and prioritizing
            potential product opportunities by combining member-level information with
            comparative patterns observed across similar members.
        
            This capability could provide Growth Marketing with a more analytically grounded
            starting point for campaign targeting than broad segmentation alone, while creating
            a foundation that can become increasingly individualized as richer information
            about member preferences, needs, behaviours, and Member Individuality becomes
            available.
            '''

        }
    },

    'Key Questions': [
        '''
        Can observable member information be organized into stable and interpretable 
        representations of engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity?
        ''',
        '''
        Do these generalized constructs correspond with meaningful differences in 
        historical member behaviour?
        ''',
        '''
        Can these constructs provide sufficient analytical value to support campaign 
        targeting without requiring a bespoke predictive model for every campaign?
        ''',
        '''
        What additional information would materially improve the granularity, reliability, 
        or predictive usefulness of the resulting capability?
        '''
    ],

    'Assumptions': [
        '''
        Engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity are related but distinct 
        constructs and should not be treated as interchangeable measures.
        ''',
        '''
        The available data contains sufficient observable behavioural and relationship 
        information to develop useful generalized representations, even if it does not 
        support precise member-level prediction.
        ''',
        '''
        Historical member behaviour can provide evidence of whether the generalized 
        constructs contain meaningful signal, despite the absence of an established 
        business-performance baseline.
        '''
    ],

    'Constraints': [
        '''
        Member-level data is currently limited in granularity, consistency, quality, and 
        longitudinal depth.
        ''',
        '''

        against which model performance can initially be compared.

        '''

def print_dict(d, indent=0):
    for key, value in d.items():
        prefix = "    " * indent

        if isinstance(value, dict):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            print_dict(value, indent + 1)

        elif isinstance(value, list):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            for item in value:
                print(f"{prefix}    - {item.strip()}")

        else:
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            print(f"{prefix}    {str(value).strip()}")

print_dict(project_dict)

    is required.

            relationship or whether meaningful financial opportunity exists elsewhere or 
            remains unexplained. A consistent approach is required to construct the Current 
            Known Financial Profile and distinguish between financial opportunity that is

        },

        'Product Opportunity': {

            holdings, Engagement, Current Known Financial Profile, identified financial 
            Opportunity, and comparison with relevant peer groups.

            basis for determining where meaningful product opportunities may exist. Even where
            financial opportunity can be identified or inferred, there is currently no
            consistent approach for determining where within the member's product relationship
            that opportunity may be relevant.
        
            A method is needed to combine what is known about the individual member with
            patterns observed among reasonably similar members, while recognizing that product
            gaps or peer similarity do not in themselves demonstrate member need, preference,
            or intent.
            ''',
        
            'Opportunity': '''
            Develop a generalized and reusable capability for identifying and prioritizing
            potential product opportunities by combining member-level information with
            comparative patterns observed across similar members.
        
            This capability could provide Growth Marketing with a more analytically grounded
            starting point for campaign targeting than broad segmentation alone, while creating
            a foundation that can become increasingly individualized as richer information
            about member preferences, needs, behaviours, and Member Individuality becomes
            available.
            '''

        }
    },

    'Key Questions': [
        '''
        Can observable member information be organized into stable and interpretable 
        representations of engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity?
        ''',
        '''
        Do these generalized constructs correspond with meaningful differences in 
        historical member behaviour?
        ''',
        '''
        Can these constructs provide sufficient analytical value to support campaign 
        targeting without requiring a bespoke predictive model for every campaign?
        ''',
        '''
        What additional information would materially improve the granularity, reliability, 
        or predictive usefulness of the resulting capability?
        '''
    ],

    'Assumptions': [
        '''
        Engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity are related but distinct 
        constructs and should not be treated as interchangeable measures.
        ''',
        '''
        The available data contains sufficient observable behavioural and relationship 
        information to develop useful generalized representations, even if it does not 
        support precise member-level prediction.
        ''',
        '''
        Historical member behaviour can provide evidence of whether the generalized 
        constructs contain meaningful signal, despite the absence of an established 
        business-performance baseline.
        '''
    ],

    'Constraints': [
        '''Member-level data is currently limited in granularity, consistency, quality, and longitudinal depth.''',
        '''There is no established organizational measurement paradigm or predictive baseline against which model performance can initially be compared.''',
        '''The capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to a fully defined business requirement, making adoption and practical use important measures of success.''',
        '''Model outputs must not imply greater precision, causality, intent, loyalty, or financial knowledge than can be supported by the available data.'''
    ]
}
def print_dict(d, indent=0):
    for key, value in d.items():
        prefix = "    " * indent

        if isinstance(value, dict):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            print_dict(value, indent + 1)

        elif isinstance(value, list):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            for item in value:
                print(f"{prefix}    - {item.strip()}")

        else:
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            print(f"{prefix}    {str(value).strip()}")

print_dict(project_dict)
project_dict['Goal Setting'] = {
    
    'Business Need': '''
    Growth Marketing needs a faster, reusable predictive capability for campaign 
    targeting because the current process relies too heavily on broad segmentation 
    and generalizations when there is insufficient time to develop bespoke analytics.
    ''',
    'Articulate Opportunity':"""
    Current state: Campaign launched → results happen → limited ability to establish what worked.
    Desired state: Campaign hypothesis → target definition → pre-campaign baseline → treatment/control where feasible → campaign → outcome window → incremental measurement → learning.
    """,
   'Goal': '''
    Develop and evaluate a generalized member intelligence capability that improves 
    Growth Marketing's ability to identify meaningful member and product opportunities 
    using available data, while establishing a reusable analytical foundation for 
    increasingly granular and effective campaign targeting.
    ''',
    'Deliverable': '''
    Develop and validate a generalized analytical framework consisting of:
    
    1) an Engagement Model that describes the strength, depth, and trajectory of the 
       member relationship;
    2) an Opportunity Model that estimates the unrealized opportunity represented by 
       the member relationship; and
    3) a Product Opportunity Model that combines these and other relevant information 
       to identify potentially relevant product opportunities.

    The framework will be evaluated against historical member behaviour to determine 
    its effectiveness, limitations, and potential application to campaign targeting. 
    Findings will be communicated to Growth Marketing together with recommendations 
    for the data and operational capabilities required to improve future predictive 
    targeting.
    ''',
    'Challenge': '''
    Developing highly effective member-level predictive models requires granular, 
    high-quality, consistent, and longitudinal member information. At present, the 
    availability and robustness of this information is limited.

    Rather than creating a level of precision that the available data cannot reliably 
    support, the project will focus on developing generalized analytical models using 
    information currently available. These models will provide progressively greater 
    analytical resolution while also identifying the additional data, information, 
    and operational capabilities required to support more granular member-level 
    prediction in the future.
    ''',

    'Approach': {

        'Phase 1 - Generalized Member Intelligence': {

            'Engagement Model': '''
            Develop a generalized view of member engagement using observable product 
            holdings, relationship breadth and depth, behavioural activity, and change 
            over time.

            Explore whether these dimensions can be represented within a single 
            engagement model or whether distinct components are required. Clustering 
            is an initial candidate methodology, but the analytical objective is to 
            identify meaningful and interpretable engagement profiles rather than to 
            prescribe a specific algorithm.
            ''',

            'Opportunity Model': '''
            Develop a generalized view of the unrealized opportunity represented by 
            each member relationship.

            Explore the relationship between estimated financial capacity, financial 
            holdings, the member's existing relationship with the organization, and 
            other relevant contextual information. The model should distinguish 
            observable information from inferred opportunity and avoid overstating 
            the precision supported by the available data.
            ''',

            'Product Opportunity Model': '''
            Use Engagement, Opportunity, existing product holdings, behavioural 
            information, and relevant constraints to identify products that appear 
            underrepresented or potentially relevant within the member relationship.

            Initially treat this as a Product Opportunity or Product Gap model rather 
            than a true Next Best Product model. Historical validation will determine 
            whether identified product opportunities are sufficiently associated with 
            subsequent product adoption or growth to support a Next Best Product 
            capability.
            '''
        },

        'Phase 2 - Validation and Learning': [
            '''
            Reconcile model outputs with historical member behaviour and performance 
            to determine whether the generalized models contain meaningful predictive 
            information.
            ''',
            '''
            Evaluate performance across relevant member profiles, demographics, 
            segments, and other characteristics to understand where the models perform 
            well, where they perform poorly, and whether model design should change.
            ''',
            '''
            Identify information that is unavailable or insufficiently robust and 
            determine what additional data would be required to support more granular 
            and reliable member-level prediction.
            '''
        ],

        'Phase 3 - Capability Advancement': [
            '''
            Develop a more complete evaluation paradigm for assessing predictive 
            performance, campaign outcomes, incremental value, model limitations, and 
            opportunities for continued improvement.
            ''',
            '''
            Translate analytical findings into clear business insights that explain 
            what the models can and cannot determine and how they can appropriately 
            support campaign targeting.
            ''',
            '''
            Identify and communicate the data, process, measurement, and operational 
            changes required to progress from generalized member intelligence toward 
            more granular predictive targeting and a mature Next Best Product 
            capability.
            '''
        ]
    },
    'Success Criteria': {

    'Analytical': '''
    Demonstrate that the generalized models produce stable, interpretable, and 
    meaningfully differentiated member profiles and opportunities that can be 
    reconciled with observable historical member behaviour.

    In the absence of an established analytical baseline, initial success will focus 
    on demonstrating meaningful signal, consistency, and practical interpretability 
    rather than improvement against an existing model.
    ''',

    'Business Adoption': '''
    Determine whether Growth Marketing understands, trusts, and finds sufficient 
    practical value in the analytical capability to use it in campaign planning, 
    targeting, evaluation, or subsequent analytical requests.

    Adoption and repeated use will represent an important measure of success given 
    that the capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to 
    an established business requirement.
    ''',

    'Capability Development': '''
    Establish a clearer understanding of the data, measurement, analytical, and 
    operational capabilities required to support increasingly granular predictive 
    campaign targeting.

    Success includes explicitly identifying limitations in the current environment 
    and translating those limitations into actionable requirements for future 
    capability development.
    '''
    }
}    

project_dict['Problem Definition'] = {

    'Project Problem': '''
    Growth Marketing requires a more reusable and analytically grounded approach to 
    identifying meaningful member and product opportunities. Current targeting is 
    constrained by limited time for bespoke analysis and by gaps in the availability, 
    consistency, and granularity of member-level information.

    The project will explore whether generalized member intelligence constructs can be 
    developed from currently available data and used to support more effective campaign 
    targeting, while explicitly identifying where additional data or analytical capability 
    is required.
    ''',

    'Analytical Problems': {

        'Engagement': {
            'Definition': '''
            Represents the extent and nature of a member's observable relationship with 
            the organization across the breadth of products and services they hold, the 
            depth with which they use those relationships, their ongoing behavioural 
            activity, and how that relationship is changing over time.

            Engagement reflects the strength and activity of the relationship rather than 
            the member's financial value, potential opportunity, satisfaction, loyalty, 
            or intent. How engaged is the member in their relationship with the organization?
            ''',

            'Problem Statement': '''
            Develop a generalized and interpretable representation of member engagement 
            using observable information about relationship breadth, relationship depth, 
            behavioural activity, and trajectory over time.

            The resulting representation should distinguish meaningfully different forms 
            and levels of engagement without conflating engagement with financial value 
            or estimated opportunity.
            '''
        },

        'Opportunity': {
            'Definition': "Develop a Current Known Financial Profile for each member using available information about their financial position, holdings, and relationship with the organization, and use that profile to identify and quantify potential financial opportunity. Opportunity may include both Known External Opportunity, where financial holdings outside the organization can be reasonably quantified, and Inferred Opportunity, where financial capacity or holdings appear likely to exist but are not directly evident within available information. The model should clearly distinguish observed, estimated, and inferred information and communicate the associated uncertainty rather than treating all identified opportunity as equally known. Based on the member's Current Known Financial Profile, what financial opportunity can we reasonably identify or infer beyond their existing relationship with us?",
            'Notes':"For the Scope of this we will focus on Current Known Financial Profile, not Member Individuality, which needs to be build on its own",
            'taxonomy':'Observed with us ↓ Known externally ↓ Estimated externally ↓ Inferred / unexplained opportunity',
            'Problem Statement': """
            Our existing relationship with a member provides only a partial view of their 
            overall financial position, making it difficult to determine whether their 
            current holdings with the organization represent the majority of their financial 
            relationship or whether meaningful financial opportunity exists elsewhere or 
            remains unexplained. A consistent approach is required to construct the Current 
            Known Financial Profile and distinguish between financial opportunity that is 
            known, estimated, or inferred.
    """
        },

        'Product Opportunity': {
            'Definition': '''
            Identifies products that may represent a meaningful opportunity to deepen a 
            member's relationship with the organization based on their existing product 
            holdings, Engagement, Current Known Financial Profile, identified financial 
            Opportunity, and comparison with relevant peer groups.
            
            Peer comparison may consider characteristics such as age, geography, financial 
            profile, wealth, and other relevant observable characteristics to identify patterns 
            in the products held by members with reasonably similar profiles.
            
            Product Opportunity represents an indication of where opportunity may exist rather 
            than a prediction of member preference, intent, or likelihood to acquire a specific 
            product. Similarity to other members and the presence of an apparent product gap 
            should therefore be treated as evidence of potential opportunity rather than evidence 
            of member need or desire.
            
            Based on what we currently know about the member and what we 
            observe among reasonably similar members, where does a meaningful product opportunity 
            appear to exist?
            ''',
                'Problem Statement': '''
            Existing product holdings and broad member characteristics provide an incomplete
            basis for determining where meaningful product opportunities may exist. Even where
            financial opportunity can be identified or inferred, there is currently no
            consistent approach for determining where within the member's product relationship
            that opportunity may be relevant.
        
            A method is needed to combine what is known about the individual member with
            patterns observed among reasonably similar members, while recognizing that product
            gaps or peer similarity do not in themselves demonstrate member need, preference,
            or intent.
            ''',
        
            'Opportunity': '''Develop a generalized and reusable capability for identifying and prioritizing potential product opportunities by combining member-level information with comparative patterns observed across similar members. This capability could provide Growth Marketing with a more analytically grounded starting point for campaign targeting than broad segmentation alone, while creating a foundation that can become increasingly individualized as richer information about member preferences, needs, behaviours, and Member Individuality becomes available.'''
        }
    },

    'Key Questions': [
        '''Can observable member information be organized into stable and interpretable representations of engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity?''',
        '''Do these generalized constructs correspond with meaningful differences in historical member behaviour?''',
        '''Can these constructs provide sufficient analytical value to support campaign targeting without requiring a bespoke predictive model for every campaign?''',
        '''What additional information would materially improve the granularity, reliability, or predictive usefulness of the resulting capability?'''
    ],

    'Assumptions': [
        '''Engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity are related but distinct constructs and should not be treated as interchangeable measures.''',
        '''The available data contains sufficient observable behavioural and relationship information to develop useful generalized representations, even if it does not support precise member-level prediction.''',
        '''Historical member behaviour can provide evidence of whether the generalized constructs contain meaningful signal, despite the absence of an established business-performance baseline.'''
    ],

    'Constraints': [
        '''Member-level data is currently limited in granularity, consistency, quality, and longitudinal depth.''',
        '''There is no established organizational measurement paradigm or predictive baseline against which model performance can initially be compared.''',
        '''The capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to a fully defined business requirement, making adoption and practical use important measures of success.''',
        '''Model outputs must not imply greater precision, causality, intent, loyalty, or financial knowledge than can be supported by the available data.'''
    ]
}
def print_dict(d, indent=0):
    for key, value in d.items():
        prefix = "    " * indent

        if isinstance(value, dict):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            print_dict(value, indent + 1)

        elif isinstance(value, list):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            for item in value:
                print(f"{prefix}    - {item.strip()}")

        else:
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            print(f"{prefix}    {str(value).strip()}")

print_dict(project_dict)
