            ''',
            '''
            Identify and communicate the data, process, measurement, and operational 
            changes required to progress from generalized member intelligence toward 
            more granular predictive targeting and a mature Next Best Product 
            capability.
            '''
        ]
    },
    'Success Criteria': {

    'Analytical': '''
    Demonstrate that the generalized models produce stable, interpretable, and 
    meaningfully differentiated member profiles and opportunities that can be 
    reconciled with observable historical member behaviour.

    In the absence of an established analytical baseline, initial success will focus 
    on demonstrating meaningful signal, consistency, and practical interpretability 
    rather than improvement against an existing model.
    ''',

    'Business Adoption': '''
    Determine whether Growth Marketing understands, trusts, and finds sufficient 
    practical value in the analytical capability to use it in campaign planning, 
    targeting, evaluation, or subsequent analytical requests.

    Adoption and repeated use will represent an important measure of success given 
    that the capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to 
    an established business requirement.
    ''',

    'Capability Development': '''
    Establish a clearer understanding of the data, measurement, analytical, and 
    operational capabilities required to support increasingly granular predictive 
    campaign targeting.

    Success includes explicitly identifying limitations in the current environment 
    and translating those limitations into actionable requirements for future 
    capability development.
    '''
    }
}    

project_dict['Problem Definition'] = {

    'Project Problem': '''
    Growth Marketing requires a more reusable and analytically grounded approach to 
    identifying meaningful member and product opportunities. Current targeting is 
    constrained by limited time for bespoke analysis and by gaps in the availability, 
    consistency, and granularity of member-level information.

    The project will explore whether generalized member intelligence constructs can be 
    developed from currently available data and used to support more effective campaign 
    targeting, while explicitly identifying where additional data or analytical capability 
    is required.
    ''',

    'Analytical Problems': {

        'Engagement': {
            'Definition': '''
            Represents the extent and nature of a member's observable relationship with 
            the organization across the breadth of products and services they hold, the 
            depth with which they use those relationships, their ongoing behavioural 
            activity, and how that relationship is changing over time.

            Engagement reflects the strength and activity of the relationship rather than 
            the member's financial value, potential opportunity, satisfaction, loyalty, 
            or intent. How engaged is the member in their relationship with the organization?
            ''',

            'Problem Statement': '''
            Develop a generalized and interpretable representation of member engagement 
            using observable information about relationship breadth, relationship depth, 
            behavioural activity, and trajectory over time.

            The resulting representation should distinguish meaningfully different forms 
            and levels of engagement without conflating engagement with financial value 
            or estimated opportunity.
            '''
        },

        'Opportunity': {
            'Definition': "Develop a Current Known Financial Profile for each member using available information about their financial position, holdings, and relationship with the organization, and use that profile to identify and quantify potential financial opportunity. Opportunity may include both Known External Opportunity, where financial holdings outside the organization can be reasonably quantified, and Inferred Opportunity, where financial capacity or holdings appear likely to exist but are not directly evident within available information. The model should clearly distinguish observed, estimated, and inferred information and communicate the associated uncertainty rather than treating all identified opportunity as equally known. Based on the member's Current Known Financial Profile, what financial opportunity can we reasonably identify or infer beyond their existing relationship with us?",
            'Notes':"For the Scope of this we will focus on Current Known Financial Profile, not Member Individuality, which needs to be build on its own",
            'taxonomy':'Observed with us ↓ Known externally ↓ Estimated externally ↓ Inferred / unexplained opportunity',
            'Problem Statement': """
            Our existing relationship with a member provides only a partial view of their 
            overall financial position, making it difficult to determine whether their 
            current holdings with the organization represent the majority of their financial 
            relationship or whether meaningful financial opportunity exists elsewhere or 
            remains unexplained. A consistent approach is required to construct the Current 
            Known Financial Profile and distinguish between financial opportunity that is 
            known, estimated, or inferred.
    """
        },

        'Product Opportunity': {
            'Definition': '''Identifies products that may represent a meaningful opportunity to deepen a member's relationship with the organization based on their existing product holdings, Engagement, Current Known Financial Profile, identified financial Opportunity, and comparison with relevant peer groups. Peer comparison may consider characteristics such as age, geography, financial profile, wealth, and other relevant observable characteristics to identify patterns in the products held by members with reasonably similar profiles. Product Opportunity represents an indication of where opportunity may exist rather than a prediction of member preference, intent, or likelihood to acquire a specific product. Similarity to other members and the presence of an apparent product gap should therefore be treated as evidence of potential opportunity rather than evidence of member need or desire. Based on what we currently know about the member and what we observe among reasonably similar members, where does a meaningful product opportunity appear to exist?''',
            'Problem Statement': '''Existing product holdings and broad member characteristics provide an incomplete basis for determining where meaningful product opportunities may exist. Even where financial opportunity can be identified or inferred, there is currently no consistent approach for determining where within the member's product relationship that opportunity may be relevant. A method is needed to combine what is known about the individual member with patterns observed among reasonably similar members, while recognizing that product gaps or peer similarity do not in themselves demonstrate member need, preference, or intent.''',
            'Opportunity':'''Develop a generalized and reusable capability for identifying and prioritizing potential product opportunities by combining member-level information with comparative patterns observed across similar members. This capability could provide Growth Marketing with a more analytically grounded starting point for campaign targeting than broad segmentation alone, while creating a foundation that can become increasingly individualized as richer information about member preferences, needs, behaviours, and Member Individuality becomes available.'''
        }
    },

    'Key Questions': [
        '''Can observable member information be organized into stable and interpretable representations of engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity?''',
        '''Do these generalized constructs correspond with meaningful differences in historical member behaviour?''',
        '''Can these constructs provide sufficient analytical value to support campaign targeting without requiring a bespoke predictive model for every campaign?''',
        '''What additional information would materially improve the granularity, reliability, or predictive usefulness of the resulting capability?'''
    ],

    'Assumptions': [
        '''Engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity are related but distinct constructs and should not be treated as interchangeable measures.''',
        '''The available data contains sufficient observable behavioural and relationship information to develop useful generalized representations, even if it does not support precise member-level prediction.''',
        '''Historical member behaviour can provide evidence of whether the generalized constructs contain meaningful signal, despite the absence of an established business-performance baseline.'''
    ],

    'Constraints': [
        '''Member-level data is currently limited in granularity, consistency, quality, and longitudinal depth.''',
        '''There is no established organizational measurement paradigm or predictive baseline against which model performance can initially be compared.''',
        '''The capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to a fully defined business requirement, making adoption and practical use important measures of success.''',
        '''Model outputs must not imply greater precision, causality, intent, loyalty, or financial knowledge than can be supported by the available data.'''
    ]
}
def print_dict(d, indent=0):
    for key, value in d.items():
        prefix = "    " * indent

        if isinstance(value, dict):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            print_dict(value, indent + 1)

        elif isinstance(value, list):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            for item in value:
                print(f"{prefix}    - {item.strip()}")

        else:
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            print(f"{prefix}    {str(value).strip()}")

print_dict(project_dict)
project_dict['Goal Setting'] = {
    
    'Business Need': '''Growth Marketing needs a faster, reusable predictive capability for campaign targeting because the current process relies too heavily on broad segmentation and generalizations when there is insufficient time to develop bespoke analytics.''',
    'Articulate Opportunity':""" Current state: Campaign launched → results happen → limited ability to establish what worked. Desired state: Campaign hypothesis → target definition → pre-campaign baseline → treatment/control where feasible → campaign → outcome window → incremental measurement → learning. """,
    'Goal': ''' Develop and evaluate a generalized member intelligence capability that improves Growth Marketing's ability to identify meaningful member and product opportunities using available data, while establishing a reusable analytical foundation for increasingly granular and effective campaign targeting''',
    'Deliverable': f''' Develop and validate a generalized analytical framework consisting of: 1) an Engagement Model that describes the strength, depth, and trajectory of the member relationship; 2) an Opportunity Model that estimates the unrealized opportunity represented by the member relationship; and 3) a Product Opportunity Model that combines these and other relevant information  to identify potentially relevant product opportunities. The framework will be evaluated against historical member behaviour to determine its effectiveness, limitations, and potential application to campaign targeting. Findings will be communicated to Growth Marketing together with recommendations for the data and operational capabilities required to improve future predictive targeting.''',
    'Challenge': '''
    Developing highly effective member-level predictive models requires granular, 
    high-quality, consistent, and longitudinal member information. At present, the 
    availability and robustness of this information is limited.

    Rather than creating a level of precision that the available data cannot reliably 
    support, the project will focus on developing generalized analytical models using 
    information currently available. These models will provide progressively greater 
    analytical resolution while also identifying the additional data, information, 
    and operational capabilities required to support more granular member-level 
    prediction in the future.
    ''',

    'Approach': {

        'Phase 1 - Generalized Member Intelligence': {

            'Engagement Model': '''
            Develop a generalized view of member engagement using observable product 
            holdings, relationship breadth and depth, behavioural activity, and change 
            over time.

            Explore whether these dimensions can be represented within a single 
            engagement model or whether distinct components are required. Clustering 
            is an initial candidate methodology, but the analytical objective is to 
            identify meaningful and interpretable engagement profiles rather than to 
            prescribe a specific algorithm.
            ''',

            'Opportunity Model': '''
            Develop a generalized view of the unrealized opportunity represented by 
            each member relationship.

            Explore the relationship between estimated financial capacity, financial 
            holdings, the member's existing relationship with the organization, and 
            other relevant contextual information. The model should distinguish 
            observable information from inferred opportunity and avoid overstating 
            the precision supported by the available data.
            ''',

            'Product Opportunity Model': '''
            Use Engagement, Opportunity, existing product holdings, behavioural 
            information, and relevant constraints to identify products that appear 
            underrepresented or potentially relevant within the member relationship.

            Initially treat this as a Product Opportunity or Product Gap model rather 
            than a true Next Best Product model. Historical validation will determine 
            whether identified product opportunities are sufficiently associated with 
            subsequent product adoption or growth to support a Next Best Product 
            capability.
            '''
        },

        'Phase 2 - Validation and Learning': [
            '''
            Reconcile model outputs with historical member behaviour and performance 
            to determine whether the generalized models contain meaningful predictive 
            information.
            ''',
            '''
            Evaluate performance across relevant member profiles, demographics, 
            segments, and other characteristics to understand where the models perform 
            well, where they perform poorly, and whether model design should change.
            ''',
            '''
            Identify information that is unavailable or insufficiently robust and 
            determine what additional data would be required to support more granular 
            and reliable member-level prediction.
            '''
        ],

        'Phase 3 - Capability Advancement': [
            '''
            Develop a more complete evaluation paradigm for assessing predictive 
            performance, campaign outcomes, incremental value, model limitations, and 
            opportunities for continued improvement.
            ''',
            '''
            Translate analytical findings into clear business insights that explain 
            what the models can and cannot determine and how they can appropriately 
            support campaign targeting.
            ''',
            '''
            Identify and communicate the data, process, measurement, and operational 
            changes required to progress from generalized member intelligence toward 
            more granular predictive targeting and a mature Next Best Product 
            capability.
            '''
        ]
    },
    'Success Criteria': {

    'Analytical': '''
    Demonstrate that the generalized models produce stable, interpretable, and 
    meaningfully differentiated member profiles and opportunities that can be 
    reconciled with observable historical member behaviour.

    In the absence of an established analytical baseline, initial success will focus 
    on demonstrating meaningful signal, consistency, and practical interpretability 
    rather than improvement against an existing model.
    ''',

    'Business Adoption': '''
    Determine whether Growth Marketing understands, trusts, and finds sufficient 
    practical value in the analytical capability to use it in campaign planning, 
    targeting, evaluation, or subsequent analytical requests.

    Adoption and repeated use will represent an important measure of success given 
    that the capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to 
    an established business requirement.
    ''',

    'Capability Development': f"Establish a clearer understanding of the data, measurement, analytical, and operational capabilities required to support increasingly granular predictive campaign targeting.\nSuccess includes explicitly identifying limitations in the current environment and translating those limitations into actionable requirements for future capability development."
    }
}

import textwrap

def print_dict(d, indent=0, width=100):
    for key, value in d.items():
        prefix = "    " * indent

        if isinstance(value, dict):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            print_dict(value, indent + 1, width)

        elif isinstance(value, list):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            for item in value:
                text = str(item).strip()
                print(textwrap.fill(
                    text,
                    width=width,
                    initial_indent=f"{prefix}    - ",
                    subsequent_indent=f"{prefix}      "
                ))

        else:
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            text = str(value).strip()
            print(textwrap.fill(
                text,
                width=width,
                initial_indent=f"{prefix}    ",
                subsequent_indent=f"{prefix}    "
            ))

print_dict(project_dict)

project_dict['Goal Setting'] = {
    
    'Business Need': '''Growth Marketing needs a faster, reusable predictive capability for campaign targeting because the current process relies too heavily on broad segmentation and generalizations when there is insufficient time to develop bespoke analytics.''',
    'Articulate Opportunity':""" Current state: Campaign launched → results happen → limited ability to establish what worked. Desired state: Campaign hypothesis → target definition → pre-campaign baseline → treatment/control where feasible → campaign → outcome window → incremental measurement → learning. """,
    'Goal': ''' Develop and evaluate a generalized member intelligence capability that improves Growth Marketing's ability to identify meaningful member and product opportunities using available data, while establishing a reusable analytical foundation for increasingly granular and effective campaign targeting''',
    'Deliverable': f''' Develop and validate a generalized analytical framework consisting of: 1) an Engagement Model that describes the strength, depth, and trajectory of the member relationship; 2) an Opportunity Model that estimates the unrealized opportunity represented by the member relationship; and 3) a Product Opportunity Model that combines these and other relevant information  to identify potentially relevant product opportunities. The framework will be evaluated against historical member behaviour to determine its effectiveness, limitations, and potential application to campaign targeting. Findings will be communicated to Growth Marketing together with recommendations for the data and operational capabilities required to improve future predictive targeting.''',
    'Challenge': '''Developing highly effective member-level predictive models requires granular, high-quality, consistent, and longitudinal member information. At present, the availability and robustness of this information is limited. Rather than creating a level of precision that the available data cannot reliably support, the project will focus on developing generalized analytical models using information currently available. These models will provide progressively greater analytical resolution while also identifying the additional data, information, and operational capabilities required to support more granular member-level prediction in the future''',
    'Approach': {

        'Phase 1 - Generalized Member Intelligence': {

            'Engagement Model': f'''Develop a generalized view of member engagement using observable product holdings, relationship breadth and depth, behavioural activity, and change over time.\nExplore whether these dimensions can be represented within a single engagement model or whether distinct components are required. Clustering is an initial candidate methodology, but the analytical objective is to identify meaningful and interpretable engagement profiles rather than to prescribe a specific algorithm.''',
            'Opportunity Model': f'''Develop a generalized view of the unrealized opportunity represented by each member relationship.\nExplore the relationship between estimated financial capacity, financial holdings, the member's existing relationship with the organization, and other relevant contextual information. The model should distinguish observable information from inferred opportunity and avoid overstating the precision supported by the available data.''',
            'Product Opportunity Model': f'''Use Engagement, Opportunity, existing product holdings, behavioural information, and relevant constraints to identify products that appear underrepresented or potentially relevant within the member relationship.\nInitially treat this as a Product Opportunity or Product Gap model rather than a true Next Best Product model. Historical validation will determine whether identified product opportunities are sufficiently associated with subsequent product adoption or growth to support a Next Best Product capability.'''
        },
        'Phase 2 - Validation and Learning': [
            '''Reconcile model outputs with historical member behaviour and performance to determine whether the generalized models contain meaningful predictive information.''',
            '''Evaluate performance across relevant member profiles, demographics, segments, and other characteristics to understand where the models perform well, where they perform poorly, and whether model design should change.''',
            ''' Identify information that is unavailable or insufficiently robust and determine what additional data would be required to support more granular and reliable member-level prediction.'''
        ],

        'Phase 3 - Capability Advancement': [
            '''Develop a more complete evaluation paradigm for assessing predictive performance, campaign outcomes, incremental value, model limitations, and opportunities for continued improvement.''',
            '''Translate analytical findings into clear business insights that explain what the models can and cannot determine and how they can appropriately support campaign targeting.''',
            '''Identify and communicate the data, process, measurement, and operational changes required to progress from generalized member intelligence toward more granular predictive targeting and a mature Next Best Product capability.'''
        ]
    },
    'Success Criteria': {

    'Analytical': f'''Demonstrate that the generalized models produce stable, interpretable, and meaningfully differentiated member profiles and opportunities that can be reconciled with observable historical member behaviour.\nIn the absence of an established analytical baseline, initial success will focus on demonstrating meaningful signal, consistency, and practical interpretability rather than improvement against an existing model.''',
    'Business Adoption': f'''Determine whether Growth Marketing understands, trusts, and finds sufficient practical value in the analytical capability to use it in campaign planning, targeting, evaluation, or subsequent analytical requests. \nAdoption and repeated use will represent an important measure of success given that the capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to an established business requirement.''',
    'Capability Development': f"Establish a clearer understanding of the data, measurement, analytical, and operational capabilities required to support increasingly granular predictive campaign targeting.\nSuccess includes explicitly identifying limitations in the current environment and translating those limitations into actionable requirements for future capability development."
    }
}    

project_dict['Problem Definition'] = {
    'Project Problem': f"Growth Marketing requires a more reusable and analytically grounded approach to identifying meaningful member and product opportunities. Current targeting is constrained by limited time for bespoke analysis and by gaps in the availability, consistency, and granularity of member-level information.\nThe project will explore whether generalized member intelligence constructs can be developed from currently available data and used to support more effective campaign targeting, while explicitly identifying where additional data or analytical capability is required.",
    'Analytical Problems': {

        'Engagement': {
            'Definition': "Represents the extent and nature of a member's observable relationship with the organization across the breadth of products and services they hold, the depth with which they use those relationships, their ongoing behavioural activity, and how that relationship is changing over time. Engagement reflects the strength and activity of the relationship rather than the member's financial value, potential opportunity, satisfaction, loyalty, or intent. How engaged is the member in their relationship with the organization?",
            'Problem Statement': f"Develop a generalized and interpretable representation of member engagement using observable information about relationship breadth, relationship depth, behavioural activity, and trajectory over time.\nThe resulting representation should distinguish meaningfully different forms and levels of engagement without conflating engagement with financial value or estimated opportunity."
        },
        'Opportunity': {
            'Definition': "Develop a Current Known Financial Profile for each member using available information about their financial position, holdings, and relationship with the organization, and use that profile to identify and quantify potential financial opportunity. Opportunity may include both Known External Opportunity, where financial holdings outside the organization can be reasonably quantified, and Inferred Opportunity, where financial capacity or holdings appear likely to exist but are not directly evident within available information. The model should clearly distinguish observed, estimated, and inferred information and communicate the associated uncertainty rather than treating all identified opportunity as equally known. Based on the member's Current Known Financial Profile, what financial opportunity can we reasonably identify or infer beyond their existing relationship with us?",
            'Notes':"For the Scope of this we will focus on Current Known Financial Profile, not Member Individuality, which needs to be build on its own",
            'taxonomy':'Observed with us ↓ Known externally ↓ Estimated externally ↓ Inferred / unexplained opportunity',
            'Problem Statement': "Our existing relationship with a member provides only a partial view of their overall financial position, making it difficult to determine whether their current holdings with the organization represent the majority of their financial relationship or whether meaningful financial opportunity exists elsewhere or remains unexplained. A consistent approach is required to construct the Current Known Financial Profile and distinguish between financial opportunity that is known, estimated, or inferred."
        },
        'Product Opportunity': {
            'Definition': "Identifies products that may represent a meaningful opportunity to deepen a member's relationship with the organization based on their existing product holdings, Engagement, Current Known Financial Profile, identified financial Opportunity, and comparison with relevant peer groups. Peer comparison may consider characteristics such as age, geography, financial profile, wealth, and other relevant observable characteristics to identify patterns in the products held by members with reasonably similar profiles. Product Opportunity represents an indication of where opportunity may exist rather than a prediction of member preference, intent, or likelihood to acquire a specific product. Similarity to other members and the presence of an apparent product gap should therefore be treated as evidence of potential opportunity rather than evidence of member need or desire. Based on what we currently know about the member and what we observe among reasonably similar members, where does a meaningful product opportunity appear to exist?",
            'Problem Statement': "Existing product holdings and broad member characteristics provide an incomplete basis for determining where meaningful product opportunities may exist. Even where financial opportunity can be identified or inferred, there is currently no consistent approach for determining where within the member's product relationship that opportunity may be relevant. A method is needed to combine what is known about the individual member with patterns observed among reasonably similar members, while recognizing that product gaps or peer similarity do not in themselves demonstrate member need, preference, or intent.",
            'Opportunity':"Develop a generalized and reusable capability for identifying and prioritizing potential product opportunities by combining member-level information with comparative patterns observed across similar members. This capability could provide Growth Marketing with a more analytically grounded starting point for campaign targeting than broad segmentation alone, while creating a foundation that can become increasingly individualized as richer information about member preferences, needs, behaviours, and Member Individuality becomes available."
        }
    },

    'Key Questions': [
        "Can observable member information be organized into stable and interpretable representations of engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity?",
        "Do these generalized constructs correspond with meaningful differences in historical member behaviour?",
        "Can these constructs provide sufficient analytical value to support campaign targeting without requiring a bespoke predictive model for every campaign?",
        "What additional information would materially improve the granularity, reliability, or predictive usefulness of the resulting capability?"
    ],

    'Assumptions': [
        "Engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity are related but distinct constructs and should not be treated as interchangeable measures.",
        "The available data contains sufficient observable behavioural and relationship information to develop useful generalized representations, even if it does not support precise member-level prediction.",
        "Historical member behaviour can provide evidence of whether the generalized constructs contain meaningful signal, despite the absence of an established business-performance baseline."
    ],

    'Constraints': [
        "Member-level data is currently limited in granularity, consistency, quality, and longitudinal depth.",
        "There is no established organizational measurement paradigm or predictive baseline against which model performance can initially be compared.",
        "The capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to a fully defined business requirement, making adoption and practical use important measures of success.",
        "Model outputs must not imply greater precision, causality, intent, loyalty, or financial knowledge than can be supported by the available data."
    ]
}
import textwrap

def print_dict(d, indent=0, width=100):
    for key, value in d.items():
        prefix = "    " * indent

        if isinstance(value, dict):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            print_dict(value, indent + 1, width)

        elif isinstance(value, list):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            for item in value:
                text = str(item).strip()
                print(textwrap.fill(
                    text,
                    width=width,
                    initial_indent=f"{prefix}    - ",
                    subsequent_indent=f"{prefix}      "
                ))

        else:
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            text = str(value).strip()
            print(textwrap.fill(
                text,
                width=width,
                initial_indent=f"{prefix}    ",
                subsequent_indent=f"{prefix}    "
            ))

print_dict(project_dict)

project_dict['Goal Setting'] = {
    
    'Business Need': '''Growth Marketing needs a faster, reusable predictive capability for campaign targeting because the current process relies too heavily on broad segmentation and generalizations when there is insufficient time to develop bespoke analytics.''',
    'Articulate Opportunity':""" Current state: Campaign launched → results happen → limited ability to establish what worked. Desired state: Campaign hypothesis → target definition → pre-campaign baseline → treatment/control where feasible → campaign → outcome window → incremental measurement → learning. """,
    'Goal': ''' Develop and evaluate a generalized member intelligence capability that improves Growth Marketing's ability to identify meaningful member and product opportunities using available data, while establishing a reusable analytical foundation for increasingly granular and effective campaign targeting''',
    'Deliverable': f''' Develop and validate a generalized analytical framework consisting of: 1) an Engagement Model that describes the strength, depth, and trajectory of the member relationship; 2) an Opportunity Model that estimates the unrealized opportunity represented by the member relationship; and 3) a Product Opportunity Model that combines these and other relevant information  to identify potentially relevant product opportunities. The framework will be evaluated against historical member behaviour to determine its effectiveness, limitations, and potential application to campaign targeting. Findings will be communicated to Growth Marketing together with recommendations for the data and operational capabilities required to improve future predictive targeting.''',
    'Challenge': '''Developing highly effective member-level predictive models requires granular, high-quality, consistent, and longitudinal member information. At present, the availability and robustness of this information is limited. Rather than creating a level of precision that the available data cannot reliably support, the project will focus on developing generalized analytical models using information currently available. These models will provide progressively greater analytical resolution while also identifying the additional data, information, and operational capabilities required to support more granular member-level prediction in the future''',
    'Approach': {

        'Phase 1 - Generalized Member Intelligence': {
            'Engagement Model': f'''Develop a generalized view of member engagement using observable product holdings, relationship breadth and depth, behavioural activity, and change over time.\nExplore whether these dimensions can be represented within a single engagement model or whether distinct components are required. Clustering is an initial candidate methodology, but the analytical objective is to identify meaningful and interpretable engagement profiles rather than to prescribe a specific algorithm.''',
            'Opportunity Model': f'''Develop a generalized view of the unrealized opportunity represented by each member relationship.\nExplore the relationship between estimated financial capacity, financial holdings, the member's existing relationship with the organization, and other relevant contextual information. The model should distinguish observable information from inferred opportunity and avoid overstating the precision supported by the available data.''',
            'Product Opportunity Model': f'''Use Engagement, Opportunity, existing product holdings, behavioural information, and relevant constraints to identify products that appear underrepresented or potentially relevant within the member relationship.\nInitially treat this as a Product Opportunity or Product Gap model rather than a true Next Best Product model. Historical validation will determine whether identified product opportunities are sufficiently associated with subsequent product adoption or growth to support a Next Best Product capability.'''
        },
        'Phase 2 - Validation and Learning': [
            '''Reconcile model outputs with historical member behaviour and performance to determine whether the generalized models contain meaningful predictive information.''',
            '''Evaluate performance across relevant member profiles, demographics, segments, and other characteristics to understand where the models perform well, where they perform poorly, and whether model design should change.''',
            ''' Identify information that is unavailable or insufficiently robust and determine what additional data would be required to support more granular and reliable member-level prediction.'''
        ],

        'Phase 3 - Capability Advancement': [
            '''Develop a more complete evaluation paradigm for assessing predictive performance, campaign outcomes, incremental value, model limitations, and opportunities for continued improvement.''',
            '''Translate analytical findings into clear business insights that explain what the models can and cannot determine and how they can appropriately support campaign targeting.''',
            '''Identify and communicate the data, process, measurement, and operational changes required to progress from generalized member intelligence toward more granular predictive targeting and a mature Next Best Product capability.'''
        ]
    },
    'Success Criteria': {

    'Analytical': f'''Demonstrate that the generalized models produce stable, interpretable, and meaningfully differentiated member profiles and opportunities that can be reconciled with observable historical member behaviour.\nIn the absence of an established analytical baseline, initial success will focus on demonstrating meaningful signal, consistency, and practical interpretability rather than improvement against an existing model.''',
    'Business Adoption': f'''Determine whether Growth Marketing understands, trusts, and finds sufficient practical value in the analytical capability to use it in campaign planning, targeting, evaluation, or subsequent analytical requests. \nAdoption and repeated use will represent an important measure of success given that the capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to an established business requirement.''',
    'Capability Development': f"Establish a clearer understanding of the data, measurement, analytical, and operational capabilities required to support increasingly granular predictive campaign targeting.\nSuccess includes explicitly identifying limitations in the current environment and translating those limitations into actionable requirements for future capability development."
    }
}    

project_dict['Problem Definition'] = {
    'Project Problem': f"Growth Marketing requires a more reusable and analytically grounded approach to identifying meaningful member and product opportunities. Current targeting is constrained by limited time for bespoke analysis and by gaps in the availability, consistency, and granularity of member-level information.\nThe project will explore whether generalized member intelligence constructs can be developed from currently available data and used to support more effective campaign targeting, while explicitly identifying where additional data or analytical capability is required.",
    'Analytical Problems': {

        'Engagement': {
            'Definition': "Represents the extent and nature of a member's observable relationship with the organization across the breadth of products and services they hold, the depth with which they use those relationships, their ongoing behavioural activity, and how that relationship is changing over time. Engagement reflects the strength and activity of the relationship rather than the member's financial value, potential opportunity, satisfaction, loyalty, or intent. How engaged is the member in their relationship with the organization?",
            'Problem Statement': f"Develop a generalized and interpretable representation of member engagement using observable information about relationship breadth, relationship depth, behavioural activity, and trajectory over time.\nThe resulting representation should distinguish meaningfully different forms and levels of engagement without conflating engagement with financial value or estimated opportunity."
        },
        'Opportunity': {
            'Definition': "Develop a Current Known Financial Profile for each member using available information about their financial position, holdings, and relationship with the organization, and use that profile to identify and quantify potential financial opportunity. Opportunity may include both Known External Opportunity, where financial holdings outside the organization can be reasonably quantified, and Inferred Opportunity, where financial capacity or holdings appear likely to exist but are not directly evident within available information. The model should clearly distinguish observed, estimated, and inferred information and communicate the associated uncertainty rather than treating all identified opportunity as equally known. Based on the member's Current Known Financial Profile, what financial opportunity can we reasonably identify or infer beyond their existing relationship with us?",
            'Notes':"For the Scope of this we will focus on Current Known Financial Profile, not Member Individuality, which needs to be build on its own",
            'taxonomy':'Observed with us ↓ Known externally ↓ Estimated externally ↓ Inferred / unexplained opportunity',
            'Problem Statement': "Our existing relationship with a member provides only a partial view of their overall financial position, making it difficult to determine whether their current holdings with the organization represent the majority of their financial relationship or whether meaningful financial opportunity exists elsewhere or remains unexplained. A consistent approach is required to construct the Current Known Financial Profile and distinguish between financial opportunity that is known, estimated, or inferred."
        },
        'Product Opportunity': {
            'Definition': "Identifies products that may represent a meaningful opportunity to deepen a member's relationship with the organization based on their existing product holdings, Engagement, Current Known Financial Profile, identified financial Opportunity, and comparison with relevant peer groups. Peer comparison may consider characteristics such as age, geography, financial profile, wealth, and other relevant observable characteristics to identify patterns in the products held by members with reasonably similar profiles. Product Opportunity represents an indication of where opportunity may exist rather than a prediction of member preference, intent, or likelihood to acquire a specific product. Similarity to other members and the presence of an apparent product gap should therefore be treated as evidence of potential opportunity rather than evidence of member need or desire. Based on what we currently know about the member and what we observe among reasonably similar members, where does a meaningful product opportunity appear to exist?",
            'Problem Statement': "Existing product holdings and broad member characteristics provide an incomplete basis for determining where meaningful product opportunities may exist. Even where financial opportunity can be identified or inferred, there is currently no consistent approach for determining where within the member's product relationship that opportunity may be relevant. A method is needed to combine what is known about the individual member with patterns observed among reasonably similar members, while recognizing that product gaps or peer similarity do not in themselves demonstrate member need, preference, or intent.",
            'Opportunity':"Develop a generalized and reusable capability for identifying and prioritizing potential product opportunities by combining member-level information with comparative patterns observed across similar members. This capability could provide Growth Marketing with a more analytically grounded starting point for campaign targeting than broad segmentation alone, while creating a foundation that can become increasingly individualized as richer information about member preferences, needs, behaviours, and Member Individuality becomes available."
        }
    },
    'Key Questions': [
        "Can observable member information be organized into stable and interpretable representations of engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity?",
        "Do these generalized constructs correspond with meaningful differences in historical member behaviour?",
        "Can these constructs provide sufficient analytical value to support campaign targeting without requiring a bespoke predictive model for every campaign?",
        "What additional information would materially improve the granularity, reliability, or predictive usefulness of the resulting capability?"
    ],

    'Assumptions': [
        "Engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity are related but distinct constructs and should not be treated as interchangeable measures.",
        "The available data contains sufficient observable behavioural and relationship information to develop useful generalized representations, even if it does not support precise member-level prediction.",
        "Historical member behaviour can provide evidence of whether the generalized constructs contain meaningful signal, despite the absence of an established business-performance baseline."
    ],

    'Constraints': [
        "Member-level data is currently limited in granularity, consistency, quality, and longitudinal depth.",
        "There is no established organizational measurement paradigm or predictive baseline against which model performance can initially be compared.",
        "The capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to a fully defined business requirement, making adoption and practical use important measures of success.",
        "Model outputs must not imply greater precision, causality, intent, loyalty, or financial knowledge than can be supported by the available data."
    ]
}

import textwrap

def print_dict(d, indent=0, width=100):
    for key, value in d.items():
        prefix = "    " * indent

        if isinstance(value, dict):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            print_dict(value, indent + 1, width)

        elif isinstance(value, list):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            for item in value:
                text = str(item).strip()
                print(textwrap.fill(
                    text,
                    width=width,
                    initial_indent=f"{prefix}    - ",
                    subsequent_indent=f"{prefix}      "
                ))

        else:
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            text = str(value).strip()
            print(textwrap.fill(
                text,
                width=width,
                initial_indent=f"{prefix}    ",
                subsequent_indent=f"{prefix}    "
            ))

print_dict(project_dict)

Based on what we currently know about the member and what we observe among reasonably similar members, where does a meaningful product opportunity appear to exist?",
            'Problem Statement': "Existing product holdings and broad member characteristics provide an incomplete basis for determining where meaningful product opportunities may exist. Even where financial opportunity can be identified or inferred, there is currently no consistent approach for determining where within the member's product relationship that opportunity may be relevant. A method is needed to combine what is known about the individual member with patterns observed among reasonably similar members, while recognizing that product gaps or peer similarity do not in themselves demonstrate member need, preference, or intent.",
            'Opportunity':"Develop a generalized and reusable capability for identifying and prioritizing potential product opportunities by combining member-level information with comparative patterns observed across similar members. This capability could provide Growth Marketing with a more analytically grounded starting point for campaign targeting than broad segmentation alone, while creating a foundation that can become increasingly individualized as richer information about member preferences, needs, behaviours, and Member Individuality becomes available."
        }
    },
    'Key Questions': [
        "Can observable member information be organized into stable and interpretable representations of engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity?",
        "Do these generalized constructs correspond with meaningful differences in historical member behaviour?",
        "Can these constructs provide sufficient analytical value to support campaign targeting without requiring a bespoke predictive model for every campaign?",
        "What additional information would materially improve the granularity, reliability, or predictive usefulness of the resulting capability?"
    ],

    'Assumptions': [
        "Engagement, opportunity, and product opportunity are related but distinct constructs and should not be treated as interchangeable measures.",
        "The available data contains sufficient observable behavioural and relationship information to develop useful generalized representations, even if it does not support precise member-level prediction.",
        "Historical member behaviour can provide evidence of whether the generalized constructs contain meaningful signal, despite the absence of an established business-performance baseline."
    ],

    'Constraints': [
        "Member-level data is currently limited in granularity, consistency, quality, and longitudinal depth.",
        "There is no established organizational measurement paradigm or predictive baseline against which model performance can initially be compared.",
        "The capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to a fully defined business requirement, making adoption and practical use important measures of success.",
        "Model outputs must not imply greater precision, causality, intent, loyalty, or financial knowledge than can be supported by the available data."
    ]

project_dict['Goal Setting'] = {
    
    'Business Need': '''Growth Marketing needs a faster, reusable predictive capability for campaign targeting because the current process relies too heavily on broad segmentation and generalizations when there is insufficient time to develop bespoke analytics.''',
    'Articulate Opportunity':""" Current state: Campaign launched → results happen → limited ability to establish what worked. Desired state: Campaign hypothesis → target definition → pre-campaign baseline → treatment/control where feasible → campaign → outcome window → incremental measurement → learning. """,
    'Goal': ''' Develop and evaluate a generalized member intelligence capability that improves Growth Marketing's ability to identify meaningful member and product opportunities using available data, while establishing a reusable analytical foundation for increasingly granular and effective campaign targeting''',
    'Deliverable': f''' Develop and validate a generalized analytical framework consisting of: 1) an Engagement Model that describes the strength, depth, and trajectory of the member relationship; 2) an Opportunity Model that estimates the unrealized opportunity represented by the member relationship; and 3) a Product Opportunity Model that combines these and other relevant information  to identify potentially relevant product opportunities. The framework will be evaluated against historical member behaviour to determine its effectiveness, limitations, and potential application to campaign targeting. Findings will be communicated to Growth Marketing together with recommendations for the data and operational capabilities required to improve future predictive targeting.''',
    'Challenge': '''Developing highly effective member-level predictive models requires granular, high-quality, consistent, and longitudinal member information. At present, the availability and robustness of this information is limited. Rather than creating a level of precision that the available data cannot reliably support, the project will focus on developing generalized analytical models using information currently available. These models will provide progressively greater analytical resolution while also identifying the additional data, information, and operational capabilities required to support more granular member-level prediction in the future''',
    'Approach': {

        'Phase 1 - Generalized Member Intelligence': {
            'Engagement Model': f'''Develop a generalized view of member engagement using observable product holdings, relationship breadth and depth, behavioural activity, and change over time.\nExplore whether these dimensions can be represented within a single engagement model or whether distinct components are required. Clustering is an initial candidate methodology, but the analytical objective is to identify meaningful and interpretable engagement profiles rather than to prescribe a specific algorithm.''',
            'Opportunity Model': f'''Develop a generalized view of the unrealized opportunity represented by each member relationship.\nExplore the relationship between estimated financial capacity, financial holdings, the member's existing relationship with the organization, and other relevant contextual information. The model should distinguish observable information from inferred opportunity and avoid overstating the precision supported by the available data.''',
            'Product Opportunity Model': f'''Use Engagement, Opportunity, existing product holdings, behavioural information, and relevant constraints to identify products that appear underrepresented or potentially relevant within the member relationship.\nInitially treat this as a Product Opportunity or Product Gap model rather than a true Next Best Product model. Historical validation will determine whether identified product opportunities are sufficiently associated with subsequent product adoption or growth to support a Next Best Product capability.'''
        },
        'Phase 2 - Validation and Learning': [
            '''Reconcile model outputs with historical member behaviour and performance to determine whether the generalized models contain meaningful predictive information.''',
            '''Evaluate performance across relevant member profiles, demographics, segments, and other characteristics to understand where the models perform well, where they perform poorly, and whether model design should change.''',
            ''' Identify information that is unavailable or insufficiently robust and determine what additional data would be required to support more granular and reliable member-level prediction.'''
        ],

        'Phase 3 - Capability Advancement': [
            '''Develop a more complete evaluation paradigm for assessing predictive performance, campaign outcomes, incremental value, model limitations, and opportunities for continued improvement.''',
            '''Translate analytical findings into clear business insights that explain what the models can and cannot determine and how they can appropriately support campaign targeting.''',
            '''Identify and communicate the data, process, measurement, and operational changes required to progress from generalized member intelligence toward more granular predictive targeting and a mature Next Best Product capability.'''
        ]
    },
    'Success Criteria': {

    'Analytical': f'''Demonstrate that the generalized models produce stable, interpretable, and meaningfully differentiated member profiles and opportunities that can be reconciled with observable historical member behaviour.\nIn the absence of an established analytical baseline, initial success will focus on demonstrating meaningful signal, consistency, and practical interpretability rather than improvement against an existing model.''',
    'Business Adoption': f'''Determine whether Growth Marketing understands, trusts, and finds sufficient practical value in the analytical capability to use it in campaign planning, targeting, evaluation, or subsequent analytical requests. \nAdoption and repeated use will represent an important measure of success given that the capability is being developed proactively rather than in response to an established business requirement.''',
    'Capability Development': f"Establish a clearer understanding of the data, measurement, analytical, and operational capabilities required to support increasingly granular predictive campaign targeting.\nSuccess includes explicitly identifying limitations in the current environment and translating those limitations into actionable requirements for future capability development."
    }
}    

project_dict['Problem Definition'] = {
    'Project Problem': f"Growth Marketing requires a more reusable and analytically grounded approach to identifying meaningful member and product opportunities. Current targeting is constrained by limited time for bespoke analysis and by gaps in the availability, consistency, and granularity of member-level information.\nThe project will explore whether generalized member intelligence constructs can be developed from currently available data and used to support more effective campaign targeting, while explicitly identifying where additional data or analytical capability is required.",
    'Analytical Problems': {

        'Engagement': {
            'Definition': "Represents the extent and nature of a member's observable relationship with the organization across the breadth of products and services they hold, the depth with which they use those relationships, their ongoing behavioural activity, and how that relationship is changing over time. Engagement reflects the strength and activity of the relationship rather than the member's financial value, potential opportunity, satisfaction, loyalty, or intent. How engaged is the member in their relationship with the organization?",
            'Problem Statement': f"Develop a generalized and interpretable representation of member engagement using observable information about relationship breadth, relationship depth, behavioural activity, and trajectory over time.\nThe resulting representation should distinguish meaningfully different forms and levels of engagement without conflating engagement with financial value or estimated opportunity."
        },
        'Opportunity': {
            'Definition': "Develop a Current Known Financial Profile for each member using available information about their financial position, holdings, and relationship with the organization, and use that profile to identify and quantify potential financial opportunity. Opportunity may include both Known External Opportunity, where financial holdings outside the organization can be reasonably quantified, and Inferred Opportunity, where financial capacity or holdings appear likely to exist but are not directly evident within available information. The model should clearly distinguish observed, estimated, and inferred information and communicate the associated uncertainty rather than treating all identified opportunity as equally known. Based on the member's Current Known Financial Profile, what financial opportunity can we reasonably identify or infer beyond their existing relationship with us?",
            'Notes':"For the Scope of this we will focus on Current Known Financial Profile, not Member Individuality, which needs to be build on its own",
            'taxonomy':'Observed with us ↓ Known externally ↓ Estimated externally ↓ Inferred / unexplained opportunity',
            'Problem Statement': "Our existing relationship with a member provides only a partial view of their overall financial position, making it difficult to determine whether their current holdings with the organization represent the majority of their financial relationship or whether meaningful financial opportunity exists elsewhere or remains unexplained. A consistent approach is required to construct the Current Known Financial Profile and distinguish between financial opportunity that is known, estimated, or inferred."
        },
        'Product Opportunity': {
            'Definition': "Identifies products that may represent a meaningful opportunity to deepen a member's relationship with the organization based on their existing product holdings, Engagement, Current Known Financial Profile, identified financial Opportunity, and comparison with relevant peer groups. Product Opportunity represents an indication of where opportunity may exist rather than a prediction of member preference, intent, or likelihood to acquire a specific product.         }

import textwrap

def print_dict(d, indent=0, width=100):
    for key, value in d.items():
        prefix = "    " * indent

        if isinstance(value, dict):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            print_dict(value, indent + 1, width)

        elif isinstance(value, list):
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            for item in value:
                text = str(item).strip()
                print(textwrap.fill(
                    text,
                    width=width,
                    initial_indent=f"{prefix}    - ",
                    subsequent_indent=f"{prefix}      "
                ))

        else:
            print(f"\n{prefix}{key}")
            text = str(value).strip()
            print(textwrap.fill(
                text,
                width=width,
                initial_indent=f"{prefix}    ",
                subsequent_indent=f"{prefix}    "
            ))

print_dict(project_dict)
engagement_dict

defintion_dict = {
'Member Engagement':"Represents the extent and nature of a member's observable relationship with the organization across the breadth of products and services they hold, the depth with which they use those relationships, their ongoing behavioural activity, and how that relationship is changing over time. Engagement reflects the strength and activity of the relationship rather than the member's financial value, potential opportunity, satisfaction, loyalty, or intent. How engaged is the member in their relationship with the organization?",
"Relationship Breadth":"The extent to which a member maintains relationships across different products, services, and capabilities offered by the organization. Breadth reflects the range of the relationship rather than the financial size or intensity of any individual component. How broad is the member's relationship with us?",
'Relationship Depth':"The extent to which the member meaningfully uses or maintains the products and services they hold. Depth distinguishes merely having a relationship from having a substantive relationship within those products or services. How deeply does the member use what they have with us?",
'Behaviour':"The frequency, consistency, and nature of the member's observable interactions with the organization. This captures how actively the member uses the organization as part of their ongoing financial activity. How actively does the member interact with us?",
'Trajectory':"The direction and evolution of the member's relationship over time. Trajectory captures whether engagement appears to be strengthening, stable, weakening, or otherwise changing rather than relying only on the member's current state.  How is the member's relationship with us changing?",
    "Current Known Financial Profile":"Represents the member's financial position as it can currently be observed or reasonably estimated from available information, including known financial holdings, wealth indicators, and their existing financial relationship with the organization. It provides a structured representation of what is currently known about the member's financial position while explicitly recognizing that the information may be incomplete, estimated, or subject to data-quality limitations. It should not be interpreted as a complete representation of the member's wealth, financial capacity, needs, or intentions. What do we currently know about the member's financial position?",
"Member Individuality Profile":"Represents a structured and consistently captured set of information about characteristics that distinguish individual members and are relevant and appropriate within the context of the financial relationship. This may include preferences, financial aspirations and priorities, communication and relationship preferences, relevant interests and values, observable behaviours, and other factors that may influence how a member engages with the organization and makes financial decisions. The Member Individuality Profile seeks to improve understanding of meaningful differences between members while deliberately limiting information to that which is appropriate and relevant in the normal course of business. It recognizes that any structured representation necessarily provides only a partial view of the complexity and uniqueness of an individual. What relevant and appropriate information do we know about what makes this member distinct as an individual?"
}

'Behaviour':"The frequency, consistency, and nature of the member's observable interactions with the organization. This captures how actively the member uses the organization as part of their ongoing financial activity. How actively does the member interact with us?",
'Trajectory':"The direction and evolution of the member's relationship over time. Trajectory captures whether engagement appears to be strengthening, stable, weakening, or otherwise changing rather than relying only on the member's current state.  How is the member's relationship with us changing?",
    "Current Known Financial Profile":"Represents the member's financial position as it can currently be observed or reasonably estimated from available information, including known financial holdings, wealth indicators, and their existing financial relationship with the organization. It provides a structured representation of what is currently known about the member's financial position while explicitly recognizing that the information may be incomplete, estimated, or subject to data-quality limitations. It should not be interpreted as a complete representation of the member's wealth, financial capacity, needs, or intentions. What do we currently know about the member's financial position?",
"Member Individuality Profile":"Represents a structured and consistently captured set of information about characteristics that distinguish individual members and are relevant and appropriate within the context of the financial relationship. This may include preferences, financial aspirations and priorities, communication and relationship preferences, relevant interests and values, observable behaviours, and other factors that may influence how a member engages with the organization and makes financial decisions. The Member Individuality Profile seeks to improve understanding of meaningful differences between members while deliberately limiting information to that which is appropriate and relevant in the normal course of business. It recognizes that any structured representation necessarily provides only a partial view of the complexity and uniqueness of an individual. What relevant and appropriate information do we know about what makes this member distinct as an individual?"
}

"Relationship Breadth":"The extent to which a member maintains relationships across different products, services, and capabilities offered by the organization. 'Relationship Depth':"The extent to which the member meaningfully uses or maintains the products and services they hold. Depth distinguishes merely having a relationship from having a substantive relationship within those products or services. How deeply does the member use what they have with us?",

def classify_breadth(row):
    product = row['PRODUCT']
    metric = row['METRIC']
    semantic = row['SEMANTIC_TYPE']
    column = row['COLUMN_NAME']

    # Product/account presence or count tells us about relationship breadth
    if semantic == 'Structural' and product not in ['Not a Product', None]:
        return True

    # Explicit product/service/channel indicators
    if metric == 'Count' and product not in ['Not a Product', None]:
        return True

    return False

def classify_depth(row):
    metric = row['METRIC']
    semantic = row['SEMANTIC_TYPE']
    product = row['PRODUCT']

    depth_metrics = [
        'Balance',
        'Average Balance',
        'Value',
        'Average Value'
    ]

    if metric in depth_metrics and semantic in [
        'State',
        'Descriptive'
    ]:
        return True

    return False

def classify_behaviour(row):
    metric = row['METRIC']
    product = row['PRODUCT']

    behaviour_metrics = [
        'Volumne',
        'Count',
        'Average Count',
        'Value',
        'Average Value'
    ]

    behaviour_products = [
        'Debit',
        'Credit',
        'Transaction',
        'EFT',
        'Internet',
        'Email',
        'Bill Payment',
        'Bank Card'
    ]

    if product in behaviour_products and metric in behaviour_metrics:
        return True

    return False

def classify_trajectory(row):
    semantic = row['SEMANTIC_TYPE']

    trajectory_types = [
        'Diagnostic: Change',
        'Diagnostic: Trend',
        'Diagnostic: Momentum',
        'Diagnostic: Volatility'
    ]

    return semantic in trajectory_types

df['BREADTH'] = df.apply(classify_breadth, axis=1)
df['DEPTH'] = df.apply(classify_depth, axis=1)
df['BEHAVIOUR'] = df.apply(classify_behaviour, axis=1)
df['TRAJECTORY'] = df.apply(classify_trajectory, axis=1)
"Current Known Financial Profile":"Represents the member's financial position as it can currently be observed or reasonably estimated from available information, including known financial holdings, wealth indicators, and their existing financial relationship with the organization. It provides a structured representation of what is currently known about the member's financial position while explicitly recognizing that the information may be incomplete, estimated, or subject to data-quality limitations. It should not be interpreted as a complete representation of the member's wealth, financial capacity, needs, or intentions. What do we currently know about the member's financial position?"
'Member Engagement':"Represents the extent and nature of a member's observable relationship with the organization across the breadth of products and services they hold, the depth with which they use those relationships, their ongoing behavioural activity, and how that relationship is changing over time. Engagement reflects the strength and activity of the relationship rather than the member's financial value, potential opportunity, satisfaction, loyalty, or intent. How engaged is the member in their relationship with the organization?"
"Member Individuality Profile":"Represents a structured and consistently captured set of information about characteristics that distinguish individual members and are relevant and appropriate within the context of the financial relationship. This may include preferences, financial aspirations and priorities, communication and relationship preferences, relevant interests and values, observable behaviours, and other factors that may influence how a member engages with the organization and makes financial decisions. The Member Individuality Profile seeks to improve understanding of meaningful differences between members while deliberately limiting information to that which is appropriate and relevant in the normal course of business. It recognizes that any structured representation necessarily provides only a partial view of the complexity and uniqueness of an individual. What relevant and appropriate information do we know about what makes this member distinct as an individual?"

Trajectory captures whether engagement appears to be strengthening, stable, weakening, or otherwise changing rather than relying only on the member's current state.  How is the member's relationship with us changing?",
"Current Known Financial Profile":"Represents the member's financial position as it can currently be observed or reasonably estimated from available information, including known financial holdings, wealth indicators, and their existing financial relationship with the organization. It provides a structured representation of what is currently known about the member's financial position while explicitly recognizing that the information may be incomplete, estimated, or subject to data-quality limitations. It should not be interpreted as a complete representation of the member's wealth, financial capacity, needs, or intentions. What do we currently know about the member's financial position?",
'Member Engagement':"Represents the extent and nature of a member's observable relationship with the organization across the breadth of products and services they hold, the depth with which they use those relationships, their ongoing behavioural activity, and how that relationship is changing over time. Engagement reflects the strength and activity of the relationship rather than the member's financial value, potential opportunity, satisfaction, loyalty, or intent. How engaged is the member in their relationship with the organization?",
"Member Individuality Profile":"Represents a structured and consistently captured set of information about characteristics that distinguish individual members and are relevant and appropriate within the context of the financial relationship. This may include preferences, financial aspirations and priorities, communication and relationship preferences, relevant interests and values, observable behaviours, and other factors that may influence how a member engages with the organization and makes financial decisions. The Member Individuality Profile seeks to improve understanding of meaningful differences between members while deliberately limiting information to that which is appropriate and relevant in the normal course of business. It recognizes that any structured representation necessarily provides only a partial view of the complexity and uniqueness of an individual. What relevant and appropriate information do we know about what makes this member distinct as an individual?",
"Decomposition Component Requirement":'For each required component, identify the information that would ideally be needed to adequately represent it, independent of the information currently available.'
}

pd.DataFrame(t.values(),index=t.keys())

dict_df = pd.read_excel('dict_semantic_functional.xlsx')

def classify_breadth(row):
    product = row['PRODUCT']
    metric = row['METRIC']
    semantic = row['SEMANTIC_TYPE']
    column = row['COLUMN_NAME']

    # Product/account presence or count tells us about relationship breadth
    if semantic == 'Structural' and product not in ['Not a Product', None]:
        return True

    # Explicit product/service/channel indicators
    if metric == 'Count' and product not in ['Not a Product', None]:
        return True

    return False

def classify_depth(row):
    metric = row['METRIC']
    semantic = row['SEMANTIC_TYPE']
    product = row['PRODUCT']

    depth_metrics = [
        'Balance',
        'Average Balance',
        'Value',
        'Average Value'
    ]

    if metric in depth_metrics and semantic in [
        'State',
        'Descriptive'
    ]:
        return True

    return False

def classify_behaviour(row):
    metric = row['METRIC']
    product = row['PRODUCT']

    behaviour_metrics = [
        'Volumne',
        'Count',
        'Average Count',
        'Value',
        'Average Value'
    ]

    behaviour_products = [
        'Debit',
        'Credit',
        'Transaction',
        'EFT',
        'Internet',
        'Email',
        'Bill Payment',
        'Bank Card'
    ]

    if product in behaviour_products and metric in behaviour_metrics:
        return True

    return False

def classify_trajectory(row):
    semantic = row['SEMANTIC_TYPE']

    trajectory_types = [
        'Diagnostic: Change',
        'Diagnostic: Trend',
        'Diagnostic: Momentum',
        'Diagnostic: Volatility'
    ]

    return semantic in trajectory_types

df['BREADTH'] = df.apply(classify_breadth, axis=1)
df['DEPTH'] = df.apply(classify_depth, axis=1)
df['BEHAVIOUR'] = df.apply(classify_behaviour, axis=1)
df['TRAJECTORY'] = df.apply(classify_trajectory, axis=1)

def relationship_definitions(df):
    
    df['BREADTH'] = df.apply(classify_breadth, axis=1)
    df['DEPTH'] = df.apply(classify_depth, axis=1)
    df['BEHAVIOUR'] = df.apply(classify_behaviour, axis=1)
    df['TRAJECTORY'] = df.apply(classify_trajectory, axis=1)

    columns = ['BREADTH', 'DEPTH', 'BEHAVIOUR', 'TRAJECTORY']
    
    df['ENGAGEMENT_DEFINED'] = df[columns].sum(axis=1).gt(0).astype(int)
    df['ENGAGEMENT_DEF_COUNT'] = df[columns].sum(axis=1)

    gb = df[columns].groupby(columns).size()
    print(f'Distribution of Variables in Model to Engagement Definition:{gb}')


relationship_definitions(df)

def relationship_definitions(df):
    
    df['BREADTH'] = df.apply(classify_breadth, axis=1)
    df['DEPTH'] = df.apply(classify_depth, axis=1)
    df['BEHAVIOUR'] = df.apply(classify_behaviour, axis=1)
    df['TRAJECTORY'] = df.apply(classify_trajectory, axis=1)

    gb = df[columns].groupby(columns).size().reset_index().rename(columns={'index':"COUNT"})
    print(f'Distribution of Variables in Model to Engagement Definition:\n{gb}')
